# Text models: settings, call logs, and the text-model eval

## Production text calls

- **One factory.** Every text `ChatOpenAI` comes from `createChatModel` in `server/src/shared/llm/chatModel.ts`. Don't construct `ChatOpenAI` anywhere else.
- **Production runs GPT-6 only** (migration of 2026-09-27; nothing deployed until the owner merges). The factory still accepts two families, each with a pinned request shape, because the eval's comparison arms run gpt-4.x through it. Any other model name throws.
  - `gpt-4.1*` and `gpt-4o*` send `temperature`, and an output cap as `max_tokens`. Eval only.
  - `gpt-6-*` and `gpt-6.1-*` (GPT-6.1 Sol, released 2026-09-29, accepted since 2026-09-30 and no group's default; a later point release is refused until its page is read) never send temperature. They always send `reasoning_effort` (none, low, medium or high, each model at the efforts it takes: `reasoningEffortsFor` in `textModelSettings.ts`, where `gpt-6.1-sol` takes no `none`, per OpenAI's model page; `minimal` is rejected everywhere) and `prompt_cache_options: { mode: "explicit" }`, and in production an output cap as `max_completion_tokens` (which counts reasoning tokens), all through `modelKwargs`. LangChain 0.6.7 does not recognise gpt-6 as a reasoning model, so its own `maxTokens` would go out as `max_tokens`. The filter check of 2026-09-27 confirmed that GPT-6 accepts `max_completion_tokens` this way.
  - Structured output stays on the default strict `json_schema`.
- **Per-role settings** live in `server/src/shared/llm/textModelSettings.ts` (`TEXT_MODEL_GROUPS`). `productionTextModels()` in `server/src/config.ts` resolves them on first use and logs one `[LLM] text models {…}` line with every group's `model@effort`. `server/src/index.ts` calls it before anything listens, and the routes build the content filter on import, so a bad setting stops the server at startup. It is lazy so the eval, which imports `config.ts` for its storage paths, never reads production's model env. Each group reads `_NAME` and `_REASONING_EFFORT` from its own prefix and nothing else. An empty value counts as unset.

  | Group | Env prefix | Default (2026-09-27) |
  |---|---|---|
  | Custom-story setup | `SETUP_MODEL_*` | gpt-6-luna, low |
  | Template editor (AI Draft, AI Iteration) | `GENERATION_MODEL_*` | gpt-6-sol, low (since 2026-09-28) |
  | Single-player beats | `TEXT_MODEL_*` | gpt-6-luna, medium |
  | Multiplayer beats | `MULTIPLAYER_TEXT_MODEL_*` | gpt-6-luna, low |
  | Single-player switch and thread analysis | `SWITCH_THREAD_MODEL_*` | gpt-6-luna, low |
  | Multiplayer switch and thread analysis | `MULTIPLAYER_SWITCH_THREAD_MODEL_*` | gpt-6-luna, low |
  | Content filter | `CONTENT_FILTER_MODEL_*` | gpt-6-luna, low |

  - Why these: single-player turns on Luna medium; multiplayer turns on Luna low, because Luna medium's 3-player p95 was 62 s, over the 60 s cap; setup on Luna low, the only GPT-6 arm inside the setup wait cap, rated on par with gpt-4.1; templates and AI Iteration on Sol low since 2026-09-28 (the owner's templates page, round 1 as it ran: Sol better on 3 items, the same on 6, worse on none, the repeat agreeing; no player waits on it, and its timeout was sized for Sol: 150 s, raised to 240 s on 2026-09-29 after the final check's slow evening); analysis on Luna low; the filter on Luna low after the filter check (`.context/content-safety.md`).
  - A gpt-4.x `_NAME` stops the server with the variable and its replacement, for example `TEXT_MODEL_NAME=gpt-4.1-mini: gpt-4.x models no longer run production text calls (GPT-6 migration, 2026-09-27). Set TEXT_MODEL_NAME=gpt-6-luna and TEXT_MODEL_REASONING_EFFORT=medium, or remove TEXT_MODEL_NAME to use that default.` Any other non-gpt-6 name stops it too.
  - `_NAME` unset: the group's default model at its default effort, or at `_REASONING_EFFORT` when that is set. `_NAME` set: `_REASONING_EFFORT` must be set too. An effort outside none/low/medium/high stops the server; the gpt-4-era `minimal` gets its own hint. So does an effort the model does not take, since OpenAI refuses it (a 400, never retried): `GENERATION_MODEL_REASONING_EFFORT=none is not supported by gpt-6.1-sol, which takes low, medium or high: … Set GENERATION_MODEL_REASONING_EFFORT=low.` The factory's `assertSupportedSettings` refuses the same settings, so the eval can't send them through production's path either (the probe sends refused shapes as raw bodies).
  - `_TEMPERATURE` is ignored with one warning per variable.
  - Setup no longer falls back to `GENERATION_MODEL_*`, and the multiplayer groups no longer inherit the single-player ones: templates can move to Sol without taking custom-story setup along, and multiplayer turns keep their own effort.
- **Retries, timeouts and output caps.** Every production call retries at most twice (`PRODUCTION_MAX_RETRIES`), and each retry is logged. A custom `onFailedAttempt` replaces LangChain's default policy, so `retryHandler` re-applies it: no retry on 400–409, cancel/abort or `insufficient_quota`. A malformed JSON reply, and a reply cut at the output cap (`finish_reason: length`, which leaves unclosed JSON), fail inside LangChain's retried section, so they are retried too. `productionCallLimits(role, players)` in `chatModel.ts` gives each role's timeout and output cap; `AIStoryGenerator` passes the story's player count, `ContentFilterService` one player; since decision B of 2026-10-01 a turn's limits come from `beatCallLimits(story)`, which gives a single player's turn that closes a chapter the lower `CLOSING_TURN_CAP` (7,500; "Timeouts and output caps" below). The values and their derivation are under "Timeouts and output caps" below.
- **Model-free seams.** `server/src/game/services/storyTextSteps.ts` holds the exact prompt and schema of each story role (`setupStep`, `beatStep`, `switchStep`, `threadStep`, `partialTemplateSchema`), how a reply changes the story (`apply`), and `analysisBefore`. Thread resolution is `ThreadResolutionService.resolveCurrentThreads`, and choice resolution is `BeatResolutionService.resolveChoice`. None of these touch the database. Production builds its requests here, and so does the eval's `adopted` variant (production's own code, for the final check).
  - `storyTextRound0/` (eval only) is production's request builders as they stood at the `round0` prompt state (the Round 0 play fixes of 2026-09-27), frozen word for word when the carried-forward forms were adopted (2026-09-28): the four prompt services as `Round0*PromptService` and `round0Steps.ts` (`round0SetupStep`, `round0BeatStep`, `round0SwitchStep`, `round0ThreadStep`, `round0IterationRequest`, `round0PartialTemplateSchema`; schemas from core's unchanged zod instances, the story state from production's renderer, whose default sections the adoption leaves unchanged). The eval's `prod` variant builds it ("today's form" in every report up to setup round 3), and so do every variant that edits production's text (the setup and turn rounds, the Stage 3 trims, the Stage 4 rewrite), the chapter backfill, the case builders and the probe's iteration schema, so every stored request rebuilds byte for byte and no variant follows a later production change. How a reply changes the story (`apply`), the plan checks and the beat repairs stay production's. Don't edit it: a production prompt change goes to `prompts/` and `storyTextSteps.ts`.
  - **Production against the measured forms** (kept tests, 2026-09-28): `adoptedSetup.test.ts`, `adoptedPlanners.test.ts` and `adoptedTurns.test.ts` hold production's requests byte for byte equal to `setupR3`, `planV2b` (switch) and `planV2f` (chapter, since the choice-result stage of 2026-09-30; `planV2e` earlier that day, `planV2c` before), and `turnB6` (one player) or the round0 form (groups), and an exploration step `choiceResult` (a group's since the same stage, a single player's since the choice-line-sp stage), prompt and JSON schema, on stories and input matrices built in the tests; `adoptedForms.test.ts` does the same through the eval's `adopted` variant on every frozen case in the output folder (setup, iteration, switch, thread and beat cases, and the chain's premises; skipped where the folder is absent), and through `adoptedTemplate` (the template editor's AI Draft) against setup round 3's template form on every frozen setup case. The only differences are the logged ones in `tests/helpers/adoptedDeltas.ts`: the scoreboard sentences in contest setups, the kids stat examples of the setup retests (`KIDS_EXAMPLES`: measured as `setupR3b`'s, 2026-09-28), (until 2026-09-30, when every ending moved to the measured `endingStateB`) the scoreboard ending rule on a contest's ending, and (the owner's feedback of 2026-09-28) no chapter rules on a switch turn (`withoutChapterRules`: the measured switch turn's last section, SPECIAL SWITCH/THREAD INSTRUCTIONS, goes), and (2026-09-30, the playthroughs' review) the switch planner's threads that fit near the end (`withThreadsThatFit`: production's `chaptersThatFit` is never below `fewestThreads`, so at 3, 6, 7 and 11 turns left PACING says 1, 2, 2 and 3 where planV2b's ÷ 4 said 0, 1, 1 and 2, the first beside "exactly 2 beats"; a contradiction fixed unmeasured). Since 2026-10-01 the switch planner also carries the parallel-threads stage's measured line where a contested outcome's next thread settles its last stage (`withContestLastStage`: not a delta but the `parallelThreads` switch planner's line, adopted as measured, which planner v2b never carried; production then equals that variant's switch request byte for byte), and the chapter planner the challenge-results stage's measured edits (`withResultsAsOutcomes` and `withResultsAsOutcomesSchema`: not deltas but the `resultsAsOutcomes` variant's approach sentence, flavor pick's line and milestone fields, adopted as measured, which planner v2f never carried; production then equals that variant's chapter request byte for byte, prompt and JSON schema), and since the kids-turns stage of 2026-10-01 a single player's read-with-kids turn is the measured `kidsTurn` byte for byte, prompt and JSON schema, every turn kind (not a delta: `adoptedTurns.test.ts` and `adoptedForms.test.ts` read it as the measured form; it is built on production's turn as measured, so no turn delta applies on top), apart from one logged delta where the turn shows images (`withKidsImageSlots` and `withKidsImageSlotsSchema`, the review of fix 6: the measured image lines put a second image on "the third or fourth paragraph" and none on the last, which a 3-paragraph kids turn can't do; production names the second paragraph, or the third of four; unmeasured, the stage's one image-on case wrote 4 paragraphs both times); since the kids-ages stage of the same day every read-with-kids turn, every player count, is the measured `kidsAges` byte for byte (built on production's grown-up turn, so no other delta applies on top; a single player's 6-8 or ageless turn is still `kidsTurn` with its logged image places), apart from one logged delta where the turn shows images (`withKidsBandImageSlots`, the review of the kids-ages adoption: each band's picture places, 3-5 the first paragraph and the second only of three, 9-12 the first and the third, a group's 6-8 the second or the third of four, in place of the grown-up turn's "third or fourth paragraph" lines the variant was built on; unmeasured, since every one of the stage's 18 turn cases had no images, which `adoptedTurns.test.ts` asserts where the cases exist; `kidsAgesAsMeasured` gives the request as measured), and a kids setup whose youngest child is 9 or older the measured `kidsAges` setup (built on production's kids setup without an age); the variants measured before it compare with production as it stood then (`productionBeforeKidsAges`: a single player's kids turn with the 6-8 band's text, a group's as a grown-up story's); and since the owner's decision of 2026-10-01 on ending milestones one on every ending, a kids ending too (`withEndingOnlyPlayed`: after the milestone lines, `ENDING_MILESTONES_PLAYED`, only the threads that just ended get milestones and no other outcome does; `endingStateB`'s unfinished line told "as unfinished … and the story ends with it still open", its current-state rule word for word; a settled decision, unmeasured; the variants measured before it compare with production as it stood then, `beforeEndingOnlyPlayed` / `productionThen`), and (since the group-levers stage of 2026-10-01, not a delta) a group's rolled chapter step read against the measured `groupLeversB` (`productionThen` also takes its lever lines out, `beforeGroupLevers`, for the variants measured before, as production prints them), with one logged delta since the review of that day (`withOwnersRollLevers`, in `adoptedTurn`: a player whose roll the step discards, in a thread on another player's own outcome with that owner in it, gets the measured line's own "none this turn." in place of the rate line; unmeasured, the stage's one case of that shape, the estate agents' turn 20, drew no lever in any arm), and (since the short-replies stage of the same day, not a delta) every turn with the measured `shortReplies` lines, which the kept tests put on each measured form with the variant's own edit (`withShortReplies`, through `withShortRepliesLines`; `productionThen` and `beforeShortReplies` take them out for the variants measured before, and the fake-run case tests read a run's requests as production sent them before, as the stored playthroughs were), and (since the options-o2c stage of the same day, not a delta) a single player's rolled chapter step read against the measured `turnO2c`, whose base (production with its lines taken out, `optionsO2cBase`) the tests hold to `turnB6` with the deltas and the short-replies lines (`productionThen` and `productionBeforeKidsAges` take O2c's lines out, `beforeOptionsO2c`, for the variants measured before; the fake-run and round-2 replay tests read a rolled step as production sent it before, from the replayed story; a single player's 6-8 kids rolled step carries them as a logged delta on `kidsTurn`, `withOptionsO2c`, unmeasured for kids; `kidsAges`, built on production's live turn, carries them already). Since the pacing-clues stage of 2026-10-01 a late turn is `pacingClues`' (the late part's clue lines on every measured form, `withLateClues`; `beforeLateClues` in `productionThen` and `productionBeforeKidsAges`), and the planners are `pacingCluesB`'s (`withPacingStepB`, `withPacedLengths` on planner v2b and v2f; `adoptedPlanners.test.ts` also holds production to `pacingCluesRequest` and the variant's base, `pacingCluesBase`, to the planners as measured). Since the group-options stage (decision A, the evening of 2026-10-01) a group's rolled chapter step is the measured `groupOptions` byte for byte, prompt and JSON schema, whose base (production with its lines and question taken out, `groupOptionsBase` through `withoutGroupOptions`) the tests hold to `groupLeversB` with the deltas and the short-replies lines as before (`expectGroup` in `adoptedTurns.test.ts`; `adoptedForms.test.ts` likewise); the owner's-roll "none" (`withOwnersRollLevers`) is part of what it measured; `productionThen` takes its lines out (`beforeGroupOptions`) for the variants measured before, and `groupLeversBase` takes out its variety lines too. Since the scenes stage, that evening, the chapter planner where a group's picks split the players and a group chapter's opening step with several threads are the measured `sharedScenesB` (not deltas: `withSharedScenes` and `withSharedScenesSchema` put its insertions on the forms measured before; `storyTextRounds/sharedScenes.test.ts` holds production to the variant on every frozen case, and to its base, `sharedScenesBase`, on a later chapter step); `productionThen`, `productionBeforeKidsAges` and `groupLeversBase` take them out (`beforeSharedScenes`, `withoutSharedScenes`), and `playthroughs3Sent` rebuilds the third round's requests without them. Since the contest-settled stage, later that evening, the chapter planner where a pick sets a contested outcome at its last stage is the measured `contestSettled` (not a delta: `withContestSettled` puts PACING's deciding-thread line on the forms measured before in `adoptedPlanners.test.ts`, `adoptedForms.test.ts` and `resultsAsOutcomes.test.ts`; `storyTextRounds/contestSettled.test.ts` holds production to the variant; `beforeContestSettled` and the variant's base, `contestSettledBase` through `withoutContestSettledLines`, give production as it stood before, and `playthroughs3Sent` rebuilds the third round's chapter plans without it). A production prompt change fails these on purpose: change a measured form only with a measured reason, and then add the change to the deltas (or build it as a new variant and re-measure, as the owner's feedback of 2026-09-28 did with `planV2c`, which carries the chapter title's "without a number" that used to be a delta).
  - `setupStep.request(premise, players, mode, maxTurns, kind, { kids, kidAges })` is setup round 3's form since 2026-09-28 (the eval's `setupR3`, byte for byte; the text in `prompts/setupPromptText.ts`, the fields in `setupSchema.ts`): a custom story asks for one difficulty level, a template for 3–5; the story length sizes the outcome slate; `kids` (a read-with-kids custom story, from its category) gets two visible shared and two visible player stats with plain names, and since the kids-ages stage of 2026-10-01 three visible player stats where the youngest child is 9 or older (`kidAges`, which `StoryCreationService` passes as it records them: the read-with-kids setting, else the premise's age line; `OLDER_KIDS_STATS` and `takesOlderKidsBudget` in `setupPromptText.ts`, the inventory's "Three visible stats…" line and the player stats' field; the eval's `kidsAges` setup byte for byte; the eval's `adopted` setup reads the ages the same way); since the setup retests of 2026-09-28 its examples come from other kinds of story ("a picnic story might count Snacks, a pirate story Gold Coins", setupR3b's measured line) instead of round 3's "Courage, Snacks, Forest Friends", which a kids setup copied in 4 of 4 (1 of 4 on the retest). The retest's other clause, the identity clause's names "in outcomes" only, did not pass (1 of 2 Casablanca setups still wrote one set of player stats per named player) and production keeps "in outcomes and stats"; nor did the Casablanca sentence after that clause (setupR3c, 2026-09-29, below), so production sends neither (`adoptedSetup.test.ts` asserts both). The reply is written in the generation order and the request's `assemble` puts it back into the fields saved today (thread design into the guidelines, each seat's outcomes on its seat, empty milestone lists, stat groups computed, the seat roles in the plan's `multiplayerCoordination`, an empty shared list for one player); `AIStoryGenerator` does that before anything reads the reply. Template generation has no category, so no kids budget.
  - `beatStep.request` is today's turn form with the option rules (B6) on a single player's rolled chapter steps since 2026-09-28 (the eval's `turnB6`, byte for byte; group turns and every other turn are the round0 form); every ending since 2026-09-30 is the eval's `endingStateB` byte for byte, every player count (each outcome told as its milestones leave it, the game's standing of each from `endingStates.ts`, the scoreboard rule with its unfinished half; `ENDING_OUTCOME_RULE`, `ENDING_OUTCOME_KINDS` and `SCOREBOARD_ENDING_RULE` in `BeatPromptService.ts`). From 2026-09-28 until then an ending was today's form plus the scoreboard ending rule on the ending of a story with a contested outcome (one line; decided by the owner, measured by no round), a logged delta that is gone: `adoptedTurns.test.ts` and `adoptedForms.test.ts` read endings against `endingStateB`, and the options and continuity arms' base keeps the ending they ran beside (their test reads production's ending as it stood then, `productionEndingForm`). A paid run of production's own ending takes a new prompt-state tag (`adopted2`'s ending records are the ending before). B9 on production's form (`adoptedSplit`) now splits the new ending too; its stored ending records are the old one's. Since the choice-result stage of 2026-09-30 a group's chapter step where some player's thread explores is the eval's `choiceResult` byte for byte (the exploration-order line after the option types: `EXPLORATION_ORDER` and `takesExplorationOrder` in `optionRules.ts`), and since the choice-line-sp stage of the same day a single player's too (measured with production's one retry of a short reply in the loop; the first run's 3 of 20 one-paragraph replies with the line were 0 of 20 there, production's own 3 of 20). No logged delta. A paid run of production's own single-player exploration turn takes a new prompt-state tag (`adopted6`'s production records are the turn before). Since the kids-turns stage of 2026-10-01 a single player's turn in a story read with a child was the eval's `kidsTurn` byte for byte, every turn kind, and since the kids-ages stage of the same day every read-with-kids turn, every player count, is the eval's `kidsAges` byte for byte: the band of the youngest child the story records (`kidsBand`: core's `kidsBandOf` on `Story.getKidAges()`, 6-8 where none is recorded), its text in `KIDS_BAND_TURNS` (`kidsTurnRules.ts`: the context's count, the rules block for "a child aged <ages>", the shouted repeat, the text field's count, the retry's count and the image places; 3-5 about 40 to 90 words in 2-3 very short paragraphs, 6-8 the kids-turns stage's 80 to 140 in 3-4, 9-12 about 150 to 230 in 4-5), printed by `BeatPromptService` (`kidsTurnText`) and applied in `beatStep.request` (`beatSchemaForKids`), and its retry of a one-paragraph reply asks for the band's count (`beatCheckOptions`, which `AIStoryGenerator` and the playthrough harness pass to `checkedBeatReply`; `beatReplyProblem` takes the count as `textCount`, production's `FULL_TEXT_COUNT` otherwise; the eval's `adopted` request carries it as `shortTextCount`). The ages come from the story's `kidAges` (the read-with-kids setting; `readingAge` on a story saved before it). Where the turn shows images, each band's lines name places its paragraphs have before the last (6-8: the second paragraph, or the third of four, the one logged delta on `kidsTurn`); no measured request carried them (the kids-ages stage's cases had no images), so they are a logged delta on `kidsAges` too (`withKidsBandImageSlots`). `adopted16`'s production records on the kids-ages cases are the turn and setup before, so a paid run of production's own kids turn or older kids setup takes a new tag. Since the owner's decision of 2026-10-01 ("Only what was played") every ending carries `ENDING_MILESTONES_PLAYED` after the milestone lines and tells an unfinished outcome "as unfinished … and the story ends with it still open" (`ENDING_OUTCOME_KINDS`), and the beat repairs drop a milestone on an outcome the chapter that just ended didn't push, at a switch and the ending (`milestoneNotPlayed`; on the stored runs exactly the estate agents' four at turn 26, nothing in round 1, `playthroughReplay.test.ts`); a paid run of production's own ending takes a new prompt-state tag. `adopted13`'s production records on the kids cases are the turn before, so a paid run of production's own kids turn takes a new tag; the second round's stored turns rebuild as the turn before (`playthroughs2Sent`, `choiceResultRequest`), which `recordedResultCases` and `outcomeSettledCases` now read too. A paid run of production's own group exploration turn takes a new prompt-state tag (`adopted5`'s records are the turn before). Since the owner's feedback of 2026-09-28 no turn reads the chapter rules (the story's thread types and switch/thread instructions, `switchAndThreadInstructions` in the state's sections): the switch turn, the one turn that printed them, no longer does; it applies each stat's own "Adjustments after threads", which it still reads, and the two planners keep the rules. Unmeasured, as decided; the setup form's chapter-rules field already says stat changes after threads belong in each stat's adjustments after threads, so the setup text is unchanged. The setup's contest setups carry the rule's three sentences (`adoptedSetup.test.ts` lists them against the measured text). Since the group-levers stage of 2026-10-01 a group's chapter step with a player in a challenge or contest thread is the eval's `groupLeversB` byte for byte, prompt and JSON schema, a player exploring beside them included (B6's lever parts for each rolled player: the reward exception, each player's computed line, `GROUP_SHARED_AND_OWN`, `NO_DOUBLE_SACRIFICE`, and in those players' fields the reward exception and the plan's lever question `GROUP_LEVER_QUESTION`; `groupSacrificeRewardLines` and `beatSchemaWithGroupLevers` in `optionRules.ts`); a group's other turns and every single-player turn as before. The variant builds on production with those lines and fields taken out (`groupLeversBase`), and the variants measured before compare a group's rolled step with production as it stood then (`productionThen`, `beforeGroupLevers` in `adoptedDeltas.ts`). A paid run of production's own group turn takes a new prompt-state tag (`adopted17`'s production records are the group turn before). Since the review of the same day a player whose roll the step discards (in a thread on another player's own outcome, that owner in the thread: `ThreadResolutionService.rollingOwner`, the owner's roll alone deciding the step) gets "none this turn." in place of the rate line (`groupLeverLine`), keeping the lever fields: a logged delta on `groupLeversB` (`withOwnersRollLevers`), unmeasured; `groupLeversBase` takes production's lines out as production prints them. Since the short-replies stage of 2026-10-01 every turn, every player count and form (a kids band's included), is the eval's `shortReplies` byte for byte, prompt and JSON schema: one line in the text rules before "- Show, don't tell." ("The text never ends after its first paragraph: write a blank line and go on with the next paragraph, until every paragraph is written.", `TEXT_GOES_ON`, printed by `BeatPromptService`) and one in each player's text field before "- Start exactly where the previous beat for this player ended." (`TEXT_GOES_ON_FIELD`, applied last in `beatStep.request` by `beatSchemaWithTextGoesOn`, which gives each player object its own text instance, slots sharing one object still sharing one; all in `textParagraphs.ts`). Neither names a count, so every form keeps its own. Production's retry of a short reply is unchanged. The variant builds on production with both lines taken out (`shortRepliesBase`), and so does `groupLeversBase`; the variants measured before compare with production without them (`productionThen`, `beforeShortReplies` in `adoptedDeltas.ts`), and the kept tests put them on every measured form with the variant's own edit (`withShortRepliesLines`, through `withShortReplies`). The eval variants built on production's live turn (`kidsAges`, `latePacing`, the turn rounds' forms, B9's split, the Stage 3 trims and the Stage 4 rewrite) carry the lines when rebuilt now, so a re-run of one under its old tag would drift (the runner warns). A paid run of production's own turn takes a new prompt-state tag (`adopted18`'s production records are the turn before). Since the options-o2c stage of 2026-10-01 a single player's rolled chapter step is the eval's `turnO2c` byte for byte, prompt and JSON schema: `THREE_WAYS` carries O2b's stat lines after the third way and the risk-only weak example after B6's, and the lever line is `optionLeverLine` in `sacrificeRewardLine`'s place (`rewardTurn`: a reward only on the chapter's first step, after a chapter that offered none, where a stat allows one; elsewhere no reward, a sacrifice on B6's rate, a second in the chapter only for a strong reason; all in `optionRules.ts`). A group's players kept B6's rate line until the group-options stage (decision A, the evening of 2026-10-01): since then a group's rolled step is the eval's `groupOptions` byte for byte, prompt and JSON schema: each rolled player's line by the owner's rules per player and chapter in a single player's words (`groupLeverRule`, `groupLeverLine`, `groupRewardTurn`: the reward turn on the chapter's first step where that player's previous chapter offered none and a stat allows one, elsewhere a sacrifice on B6's rate, a second only for a strong reason, none, and none where the step discards the player's roll), O2b's stat lines and risk-only example under a lead for a group's rolled players after the option examples (`GROUP_OPTION_VARIETY`, printed by `BeatPromptService`), and the plan's lever question asked from those lines (`GROUP_LEVER_QUESTION`, reworded from `groupLeversB`'s so that a strong-reason line never reads as a call for a second sacrifice). The variant builds on production with those taken out (`groupOptionsBase`; the line production printed before is `groupRateLine`). `adopted23`'s production records are the group turn before, so a paid run of production's own group rolled step takes a new tag; a read-with-kids group's rolled step carries the lines too (unmeasured for kids). B9's split (`requestFormB9.ts`) moves the new line into THIS BEAT as it moved the old. `adopted20`'s production records are the turn before, so a paid run of production's own single-player rolled step takes a new tag; a read-with-kids rolled step carries the lines too (unmeasured for kids). Since the pacing-clues stage of 2026-10-01 a turn in the story's late part (past two thirds of its turns, `isLatePart` in `pacing.ts`), after the first and before the ending (`takesLateClues`), is the eval's `pacingClues` turn byte for byte: the late hint line in the hint line's place (plant no new mystery; where it fits, explain a detail an earlier beat left unexplained) and the interludes' late line after their last example (`LATE_CLUES_TEXT` in `lateClues.ts`, printed by `BeatPromptService`; `latePacing.ts` reads the same text). Every player count and form, a read-with-kids turn included (unmeasured for kids). The variants measured before compare with production without them (`productionThen`, `beforeLateClues` in `adoptedDeltas.ts`), and the kept tests put them on every measured form (`withLateClues`; `groupLeversB` and `kidsAges`, built on production's live turn, carry them already). As with the short-replies lines, the eval variants built on production's live turn carry them on a late turn when rebuilt now (the runner warns of a drift under an old tag); `latePacing`'s and `pacingClues`' late turns are production's now, as measured. `adopted21`'s production records are the turn before, so a paid run of production's own late turn takes a new tag.   - `storyTextTrims.ts` (eval only) derives the Stage 3 variants from the round0 requests (`storyTextRound0/`; "production" below means that form). Each is production's request minus the planning fields nothing reads after generation, and minus the prompt lines asking for them. Kept schema fields stay production's zod instances, shared across player slots where production shares them. Prompt edits are anchored on production's wording and apply only before the story state or premise. Each must match exactly once, so a production prompt edit near an anchor fails `storyTextTrims.test.ts` instead of silently undoing a trim: fix the anchor, not the prompt. Only `variants.ts` calls it. Delete it once Stage 3 is decided and Stage 4's rewrite supersedes it.
  - `storyTextRewrite/` (eval only) builds the Stage 4 split requests, for single-player beats (`beat.ts`; a multiplayer story throws) and custom-story setup (`setup.ts`).
    - `fixed`: the rules, each stated once across the whole request. It is byte-identical for every beat call, and for setup within the single-player class and within the multiplayer class, so it caches.
    - `perCall`: this call's branch instructions, then production's story state or configuration block (with the premise), verbatim.
    - `schema`: production's fields, types, key order and shared instances, with the named descriptions rewritten and the list counts in one of two forms. Production clauses that restate a rule the prompt carries are cut from the other descriptions (title, stat changes, milestones, new elements, facts and world building for beats; the stat lists, the stat's type, name, visibility, backgrounds flag and outcome-tracking clause, the background's multiplayer clause and the conversion rates' balance clause for setup). The tests pin each cut clause by name, since the exact-sentence check cannot see a paraphrase.
    - Counts (`RewriteCounts` in `common.ts`, a builder parameter defaulting to `exact`):
      - `exact` is Stage 4 as it ran, and stays byte-identical so its records reproduce: `minItems`/`maxItems`, several exactly 3, with the count cut from several descriptions. Under strict structured output a model that wants fewer items cannot close the list, so it pads whitespace or writes blank items (Known limits).
      - `worded` is the count fix: no `minItems` anywhere, `maxItems` only as a cap, each count in words in its description, and one fixed line (`NO_EMPTY_ITEMS`) that no list item is empty. Beats: exactly three options (an exploration step maps options to its three resolutions by index), two to four interludes (rule B32), one to three show-don't-tell points, up to two modifiers. Setup restores production's own words: 6-8 story elements (added to the description, since the fixed rules give only the mix), three facts, 6-8 thread types, 0-3 instructions, at most 3 stat groups, at least 3 effects per stat (no cap), and three conversion rates, archetypes, coordination mechanisms, identities and backgrounds. The outcome count stays in the fixed rules.
    - The with-examples setup carries production's example stat setups verbatim.
    - It reads the round0 prompt (`storyTextRound0/`) at four first-occurrence anchors: the state marker, `Number of players:`, `EXAMPLE STAT SETUPS` and `Character Selection Instructions`. A production edit there fails its tests: fix the anchor, not the prompt.

Only the words that speak of the stat count (`saidOfStat`, the same day's review finding): read whole, an option's "greatly increasing disruption" or "so more delegates can stay" and a rule's "+30 bonus" turned an ordinary stat's sacrifice into a rise, so its correct payment went unrecorded and the eval read it as "changed the other way". Over the stored outputs' and both playthrough rounds' 769 distinct sacrifice and reward options on percentage and number stats, the whole-text reading turned 11 from their kind's way, 5 of them wrongly (Personal Agency twice, Resource Credits, Media Savvy, Timeline Stability by its rule); the stat-bound reading turns the 6 right ones (Stress Level ×5, Hunger) and no other. Over the 3,483 distinct stat rules stored (eval cases, both rounds' setups, setup outputs) read alone, it drops 111 wrong directions ("to gain a boost", "a lower chance", "a paid errand", "a +30 bonus"), adds 4 right ones, and loses 6 right ones the whole reading had by accident ("a temporary spike (+20%)" with no name beside it, "an added interruption"); one ambiguous. The turn's own lines ("should lose whatever was sacrificed", "a sacrifice (losing a stat …)") are unchanged: whether a turn pays a rising sacrifice the right way is unmeasured.

Offer it only as a grouped thread, a flavor switch on it for every player, never as one direction among others: one side could take that direction alone while the other side is elsewhere, and the contest would be settled without them." (production's since the stage, below; the variant takes it out of production's request before inserting its own, so it builds as measured); (2) the chapter planner, where the players' picks set more than one outcome (`takesParallelLine`, a group after its first chapter), after "- Mixed setup: …": "Parallel threads happen at the same time, in one world. A character in one player's scenes is not in another player's scenes (a message may reach them), a player appears in another player's beat only if they are in the same thread, and a shared place (a ship, a house) is in the same state in every beat." (`PARALLEL_THREADS_TEXT`). Production's request byte for byte elsewhere (a test holds it on every frozen switch, chapter and turn case); schemas and assembly production's. The switch planner offered each contested last stage as one direction among others, the chapter planner must follow the picks and is told nothing about where parallel threads happen, and the group turn's consistency lines cover only players in the same thread or switch.
