### Timeouts and output caps

Derived on 2026-09-27 from the valid replies of the chosen arms in `DOCS/2026-09-26_gpt6-text-eval/calls.jsonl` (all prompt variants of the arm, pipeline steps included; "normal" leaves out the replies that padded whitespace). Output tokens include reasoning. Each timeout is about twice the slowest normal reply, and each cap sits well above the longest normal reply and far below the padded runaways. At the models' output speed (about 140–200 tokens/s) each cap is reached at about the role's timeout, so whichever binds first cuts a runaway, and a cut-off call, which bills what it wrote, costs at most the cap. The old timeouts were 240 s (setup, editor), 180 s (beats), 120 s (analysis) and 60 s (filter), with no cap.

| Role | Arm measured | Wait p95 / slowest normal | Longest normal reply | Timeout | Output cap | Margin |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Beats, 1 player | Luna medium, 169 production-form replies (535 over all forms) | 41.1 s / 43.9 s (57.2 s over all forms) | 5,803 tokens (6,770 over all forms) | 90 s | 12,000 | timeout 2.1× the slowest production-form reply, 1.6× the slowest of any form; cap 1.8× the longest of any form. The padded replies ran to 24,500–41,190 tokens and 121–295 s; two that padded and recovered at 8,598 and 8,975 tokens pass. |
| Beats, 2 players | Luna low, 49 | 35.6 s / 40.4 s | 5,123 | 90 s | 14,000 | timeout 2.2×; cap 2.7× (1.9× Luna medium's longest 2-player reply, 7,467) |
| Beats, 3 players | Luna low, 10 | 42.2 s / 42.2 s | 5,695 | 90 s | 16,000 | timeout 2.1×; cap 2.8× (1.8× Luna medium's 8,938) |
| Custom-story setup | Luna low, 36 production-form (72 over all forms) | 61.5 s / 63.3 s (76.0 s) | 9,646 (10,825) | 120 s | 20,000 | timeout 1.9× the slowest production-form setup, 1.6× the slowest of any form; cap 1.85× (1.6× Luna medium's 12,585) |
| Template generation, AI Iteration | Sol low (the editor's model since 2026-09-28): 81 setups, and the final check's two AI Drafts (2026-09-28) | Sol low 101.4 s / 111.1 s; the AI Drafts 104 s and 134 s, at 20-24 ms a token (10-11 on 2026-09-26) | Sol low 8,258 (9,610 at any effort; the AI Drafts 5,232 and 5,661) | 240 s (150 s until 2026-09-29) | 20,000 | timeout 2.2× Sol low's slowest setup and 1.8× the slowest AI Draft: at the slow evening's 24 ms a token, Sol low's longest setup takes about 195 s, which 150 s would have cut. Cap 2.4× Sol low's longest setup (2.1× Sol's longest at any effort). At that pace the timeout binds first (240 s is about 10,000-12,000 tokens), at 26 September's pace the cap. Iteration regenerates at most a whole template; no eval stage has run it. |
| Switch and thread analysis | Luna low: switch 204, thread 168 | thread 9.6 s / 12.8 s (switch 6.2 s / 7.3 s) | thread 1,582 (switch 803) | 30 s | 4,000 | timeout 2.3× the slowest thread plan; cap 2.5× (2.2× Luna medium's 1,782) |
| Content filter | Luna low, the filter check: 37 cases, run twice | 1.6 s / 2.1 s (both runs) | 131 | 15 s | 2,000 | timeout 7×, cap 15×: the sample is small, and the worst case stays short (2 attempts × 3 calls × 15 s = 90 s before it fails closed) |

- The multiplayer caps scale the single-player cap by Luna medium's longest reply per player count (about 1.8× each), so multiplayer can move to medium without new caps.
- A turn now fails at 90 s instead of 180 s, and with the 2 retries a stuck turn gives up after about 4.5 minutes instead of 9.
- The template editor's timeout went from 150 s to 240 s on 2026-09-29 (the decision on the final status note's recommendation): no player waits on it. A stuck AI Draft or AI Iteration now gives up after about 12 minutes (three attempts); the client's two requests (`templateApi.ts`) set no timeout of their own, and the server none on its routes.
- The eval sends no output cap and waits 300 s, so it still sees the runaways production now cuts. The one exception is a request that carries production's limits (`limits`, read by `callOptionsFor` in `executor.ts`): turn round 3's B9, whose guards are part of the form under test.
- How often production's caps cut a reply is read from the `[LLM] retry` lines' `"finishReason":"length"` (below), not from the calls' `metrics`, which only the successful attempt writes.
- The turns the caps cut in the eval: a single-player reply that reasons to the 12,000-token cap and writes nothing. The first replay (2026-09-30) saw it only on story 8988006e's turns right after its first chapter; the second (2026-10-01, `runaway-2`, below) read every stored call: it falls only on turns that close a chapter (a switch turn after a chapter, or the ending), on Luna medium, since about 29 September (15 of 192 such first tries since then, 1 of 143 before; none of 639 chapter steps and openings and 40 first turns since; none of the 145 closing turns ever sent on Luna low), on six story moments, in bursts. Each cut costs $0.0074 and about 70-90 s; every turn answered within production's two retries (one ran away on its first retry too). Since decision B of 2026-10-01 a single player's closing turn has its own cap, `CLOSING_TURN_CAP` 7,500 (`chatModel.ts`; `productionCallLimits(role, players, { closingTurn })`, read for a turn through `beatCallLimits(story)` in `storyTextSteps.ts`, which AIStoryGenerator and the eval's `adopted` arm use; every other variant keeps the cap by player count it was measured with). Set from the stored replies (a temporary probe over `calls.jsonl` and `prep-calls.jsonl`, deleted): 445 clean answered single-player closing turns on Luna medium (every form, 26 September to 1 October; four padded with whitespace left out, two of them uncapped 2026-09-26 replies of 24,817 and 41,190 tokens, one a 7,253-token retry of 1 October, one 3,781), reasoning included: median 3,442, p90 4,306, p95 4,656, p99 4,994, max 5,853 (round 2's New Avalon switch turn 16; reasoning alone at most 3,774); since 29 September 278, max 5,853. 7,500 is 28% over the longest and 50% over the p99. The 19 cut first tries stored (all single-player closing turns, 18 since 29 September) ran 56-89 s at 134-214 tokens a second (median 173): a runaway is cut 4,500 tokens sooner, about 26 s (21-34 s). On the stored playthroughs (`playthroughReplay.test.ts`): 38 answered closing-turn attempts, all under it; three attempts cut at 12,000 (round 1's New Avalon 5, round 2's mouse ending, round 3's New Avalon 17) would have stopped about 30, 21 and 29 s sooner, and round 3's New Avalon 13, which timed out at 90 s, up to 34 s sooner. Group turns keep 14,000 and 16,000 (none of their closing turns ever ran away, on Luna low). Unmeasured in play: the rate of runaways is unchanged by a cap, only their cost.

## Call and turn logs (no text, no user ids)

- **`[LLM] {…}`** comes from `llmCallLogger` in `server/src/shared/llm/usageRecorder.ts`, one line per call. It carries:
  - role, model and effort;
  - `metrics`: input, cached, cache-write, output and reasoning tokens, finish reason, a refusal flag, the served model and the service tier;
  - `ms`, and `error` {name, status, code, param, finishReason} on failure;
  - the invoke tags: storyId, turn (the turn being generated), players, beatType (beats only), images and pregeneration.

  Only these tag names pass (`tagsFrom`); any other metadata is dropped. The factory puts `role` into the model's metadata, and callers pass the story tags as `invoke(prompt, { metadata })`.
- **`[LLM] retry {…}`** is one line per retry: role, attempt, retriesLeft, status, code, name and finishReason.
  - A reply cut off at its output cap never reaches `metrics.finishReason`: with strict structured output, openai's parse throws `LengthFinishReasonError` before LangChain sees the reply, and the call is retried. So it shows only as `"finishReason":"length"` on the retry line, or in `error` when every attempt was cut off (`errorFinishReason`; `content_filter` likewise).
  - `name` is the error's class where its name is the generic "Error" (`errorName`): openai's errors set none, so a 500 reads `InternalServerError`, a cut-off reply `LengthFinishReasonError`, a timeout LangChain's `TimeoutError`. No error message is logged, since some quote the model's reply.
- **`[LLM] text models {…}`** is one line at startup: every settings group as `model@effort`, as resolved from env (`describeTextModels`). It is the first thing to check after a deploy.
- **`[TurnTiming] {…}`** comes from `server/src/game/services/turnTimings.ts`, which keeps in-memory timestamps for at most 5,000 stories. It logs three events:
  - `delivered`, with the wait since that story's last choice. It fires only when a broadcast carries a higher turn; choice and image broadcasts don't count.
  - `choice`: reading seconds (only for a choice on the delivered turn), pregeneration `complete`/`partial`/`none`, and `duplicate`. `duplicate` means the pregeneration was not complete and still running, so the live progression repeats it.
  - `pregeneration`: seconds and ok.
- Retention is the host's log retention.

## The text-model eval (`server/src/evals/textModelEval/`)

Run everything from `server/`. The harness finds `data/` and `server/.env` relative to the working directory, refuses `NODE_ENV=production`, and never touches the database.

**Commands** (`npm run eval:text -- …`):

- *(no flags)*: the dry run. It prints the cases, the jobs, and the estimated $ and minutes per stage against the caps. No API calls. It plans every row under `--prompt-state <tag>`, by default `round0` (`CURRENT_PROMPT_STATE` in `variants.ts`), so its jobs are the ones a `--run` under that tag would still send; records under other tags don't count as done. It refuses a retired tag, as `--run` does.
- `--probe [--max-spend 1]`: what Sol and Luna accept.
  - Raw SDK checks: a small strict schema at none, low, medium and high; temperature with effort, `minimal`, verbosity, explicit and implicit caching, and a cache breakpoint. Each cache check has its own prefix, so one check's cache write cannot show up as another's read.
  - Every production schema, capped at 64 output tokens: at all four efforts on Luna, at low on Sol (schema validation does not depend on effort).
  - Full completions through the production path: Sol medium, Luna medium and Luna high.
  - Checks run cheapest first. The tail is skipped at the cap.
  - Findings of 2026-09-26 (about $0.47): every schema accepted on both models; temperature 0.2 accepted at none and rejected above it; `minimal` rejected (the API lists none, low, medium, high and xhigh); explicit caching writes nothing, the implicit default writes the whole prompt; a breakpoint is read back on the repeat; cached, cache-write and reasoning tokens are all reported.
- `--build-cases [--rebuild-cases] [--max-spend 0.75]`: plays templates forward with the baseline and freezes every case. It refuses to overwrite frozen cases without `--rebuild-cases`, because rebuilding changes the inputs.
- `--run --stage 0|1-2|3|4|setup-rounds|turn-rounds|migration --prompt-state <tag>`: the replay. It refuses to start when the estimate exceeds a cap, stops scheduling when the next call would, and resumes from `calls.jsonl`. It rewrites `results.md` at the end.
  - The rounds after the Round 0 play fixes have their own stages (see Budget): `setup-rounds` and `turn-rounds` get their candidates with the eval-only variants they test (`setup-rounds` plans the setup rounds, `turn-rounds` turn round 1; see Arms), and `migration` runs production's GPT-6 defaults on today's prompts. The dry run lists a round stage's open jobs and estimate per arm under its row. These three plan no baseline job and gate no candidate on a baseline record (`stageRunsBaseline` in `arms.ts`): gpt-4.x stays out of new runs, and every stored baseline record stays the comparison.
  - Filters: `--role setup,beat,switch,thread,iteration` (`analysis` means switch plus thread), `--mode isolated|pipeline`, `--arms`, `--cases`, `--samples N`, `--subset15`, `--no-mp-continuations` (leaves out multiplayer beats other than first beats and endings), and `--rare-failure skip|only` (leaves out the rare-failure batch, or plans only it, so it can run after the analysis and the chains). The dry run takes the same filters.
  - Prompt states: `prefix` is before the prompt-bug fixes (Run A), `postfix` after them, through Stage 4b. `--run` refuses both (`retiredPromptStateProblem` in `variants.ts`): the pre-fix prompts exist only in Run A's records, and the Round 0 play fixes (2026-09-27) changed the production prompts after the `postfix` records (the state text and the multiplayer first-thread rule), so a resumed run under either tag would mix two prompt versions. New runs take a new tag: `round0` for today's prompts (`CURRENT_PROMPT_STATE`; a prompt change retires it and sets the next). Reading the old tags (pages, scores, reports) is unchanged.
  - The post-fix prompts (Milestone 2, test plan A7) fix these on today's models:
    - thread beats see their own thread's beat texts;
    - the ending sees the thread it wraps up, the outcomes, and the stats' "Adjustments after threads";
    - only switch beats are told to "create the next switch";
    - a later switch beat keeps its thread-resolution instruction;
    - single-player switch analysis gets a single-player example;
    - the premise and iteration feedback are sent verbatim in `<premise>`/`<feedback>` tags, not upper-cased;
    - the "show other players' images" note goes to multiplayer stories;
    - six separator and punctuation slips.
  - They also resolve eight contradictions:
    - the player's own portrait is allowed in the first beat only;
    - 3 interludes even with images off;
    - basePoints +5 to −15, sacrifice +30 and reward −30 (`POINTS_FOR_SACRIFICE`/`POINTS_FOR_REWARD`);
    - one difficulty level for a custom story;
    - the schema's `backgroundArchetypes` name;
    - ±20 for a major stat effect;
    - 3–5 sentences per paragraph;
    - 3–4 *visible* shared and player stats plus any invisible ones.
  - The checkers follow the corrected rules: visible stat counts, and sacrifice and reward at exactly ±30. Endings are not held to three options, because the ending prompt asks for none.
  - Paragraphs are split as the game shows them. `playerText.ts` copies the client's `normalizeStoryText` (`client/src/game/utils/storyTextProcessor.ts`), so a single newline starts a paragraph and an image line joins the paragraph after it. The paragraph, sentence and image-placement checks and the rating pages all use it. Keep it in step with the client function.
- `--rating-page setup|turn --arms <baseline,cand1,…> [--items N] [--per-item K]`: a blind rating page.
  - An arm is `armKey` or `promptState:armKey`; the first arm is the baseline.
  - `--per-item K` shows the baseline plus K candidates per item. Every K-subset appears once per cycle, the least-shown first, so with 3 candidates, K = 2 and 9 items each candidate is on 6 items. The baseline's label also shifts once per cycle, so each pair meets it at every label. Subsets are handed out in stratum order (setup: player count first), so each candidate is spread over the strata; the page still shows the items in salted order. The repeated item keeps the arms of the item it repeats. Every arm still needs a usable sample 1 on a case for that case to qualify.
  - `--cases a,b,…` limits the regular items to those cases; the baseline-against-baseline control still comes from the other cases.
  - Post-fix pages need `--prompt-state postfix` (or `postfix:<armKey>` refs), because a bare arm key defaults to `prefix`.
  - `--preview` allows a single arm and shows a banner. `--preview --stored` builds a layout-only page from stored beats and custom-story setups, with no eval output needed.
  - A setup option shows the whole design the game reads (`setupCard` in `ratingContent.ts`): difficulty, character-selection screen, every guideline, outcomes, every stat field, story elements, each player's outcomes, identities and backgrounds with starting stats, and image instructions. An item's options sit side by side as a comparison grid (`ratingRows.ts`): one column per option, one row per section and sub-section, so each section starts at the same height in every column, and a row is one `<details>`, so its header folds it in all columns at once. Below `WIDE_FROM` (`ratingPage.ts`) the rows stay and the options stack inside each, every cell tagged with its letter. Single stats, story elements, outcomes, identities and backgrounds fold one by one inside their cell, and a header shows what it hides (a list's count, a stat's name and type, an element's name; for Guidelines and Image instructions how many fields the option filled, for Stats and each player every list with its count); an option without a section the others have shows "—". Folded at load (owner, 2026-09-27; `startsOpen` and `ENTRY_STARTS_OPEN`): Guidelines and, within it, Tone, Decisions and Types of threads; every stat; Story elements and every element; each player's Backgrounds and every background; Image instructions. Nothing remembers a fold. The character-selection plan is left out, because the game never reads it. The fixed text is `SETUP_FIELD_LABELS`, `TURN_FIELD_LABELS` and `CONTEXT_LABELS`, which join the page's field labels, so the blinding word check reads it.
  - A turn option shows each player's visible beat: title, text, options, interludes. Under the options (the owner's feedback of 2026-09-29, `ratingMechanics.ts`, rows open at load), read from the stored reply after the game's repairs (`repairBeatReply`) on the story the call saw (`beatInput`: the case's, or a chain's with its own plan applied):
    - "Choice mechanics", per player: each choice's type; a challenge's risk, signed base points and stat bonuses as `BeatResolutionService` counts them (the first two, each within ±15; "written as" and "not counted" say where the game differs from the reply; an id `Story.getStatById` can't resolve after the beat repair, which since 2026-09-29 rewrites the doubled seat form `player1_player_skills_traits` as `player1_skills_traits` (`.context/story.md`), is shown as the game shows it, by the id), with the reasons players see; a sacrifice or reward with its stat, the amount its text names and the stat's own rule; an exploration choice's result (a step's Resolution n) or direction (a topic switch) by position. Nothing at an ending, which shows no options.
    - "What this turn changes": the previous choice's sacrifice or reward, if it was one, as "Previous choice sacrificed: Supplies: applied, 40 → 30" (or "not applied", "changed the other way", "written, but nothing changed"; its stat is the one its text names, else the one whose held value it names, else the only stat allowing that lever; where the text names several, the one it pays (`leverStat`): of those whose rule allows that lever, the one named nearest an amount, else the first named, and only that stat is read, so another named stat moving never reads as paid ("Burn 10 Supplies to steady your Nerve" with only Nerve changed reads "Supplies: not applied"); the option's own line names that stat too); every stat change as before → after, applied in order with the game's `ChangeService` on the case's state, flagged "Doesn't fit its stat" where it is off its ladder (`isOffLadder`), out of 0-100, a count below 0, a change the game can't apply to that type, a number written as text, a list item not held or already held, no value to change, or a stat that changes only after a chapter on a turn no chapter ended (the previous lever's stat exempt); milestones with their outcome, new story elements, introductions, facts, and what the repairs dropped. One player: after the mechanics row; a group: one row after every player, grouped Shared, then each player with that player's previous lever. Every fixed string is in `MECHANICS_LABELS`, and `metadataLeaks` now reads a turn option's own line labels (plan, mechanics, changes) too.
    - `tallyTurns(turnReadingsFor(…))` counts, per arm, previous levers by what the turn did and stat changes that don't fit.
  - Above the grid sits the item's context, shared by all options (`turnContext` in `ratingContext.ts`). It reads the frozen state with the case's fixed analysis appended, which is what every option's beat call saw: the pages hold isolated beat arms, never chains, so on an analysis turn every option got the same chapter plan, and the context says so. Its folds, in order:
    - This chapter: the turn and its kind (a switch, thread step k of n, or the ending); a thread that just ended, as the beat prompt's previous-thread block shows it: its result, the milestone this beat must add, its outcome, its type and every step with its result; the current thread's type, players or sides, the outcome it advances with its milestones, the kind of milestone, the length, the plan step by step with past results and the current step marked, and the possible results; a switch's type with its set question and outcome, or its directions and the outcome each names; at the ending, every outcome it must resolve, with its resolutions.
    - Outcomes: shared and per character, milestones so far of intended. A shared outcome that players' lists also copy shows once, as shared, since milestones land there. Badges mark the outcome that gets a milestone this turn (the just-ended thread's, on a switch or the ending) and those the current chapter advances; one outcome can carry both. Outcome lists that are present but empty say "The story holds no outcomes."
    - The chapter's earlier turns in full (the owner's feedback of 2026-09-28: continuity can't be judged from summaries; `chapterTextSections`): the switch turn that opened the chapter ("The switch that opened the chapter"), then each earlier turn ("Earlier in this chapter" on a chapter step; "In the chapter that just ended" on a switch after a chapter and on the ending, which follow that chapter's last turn), per player in order (": player N" in multiplayer), each as the player saw it: turn number and title, the paragraphs with pictures as muted notes, the option chosen and its result. A chain item's context shows its opening switch the same way. One fold per turn and player (keys `chapterText.<n>` or `chapterText.<n>.<slot>`), open at load. Nothing on the first turn.
    - Before this turn, as before (on the first turn the introduction and the chosen characters; an empty introduction or name is said to be missing).
    - Story so far: each earlier chapter with its results: a switch's question, outcome and each player's chosen option; a thread's outcome, result and planned milestone (the plan's wording; the outcome's own, more specific milestone is under Outcomes); and each beat's title and summary. Earlier chapters and turns stay summaries here.
    - The story: title, world, rules, tone, conflicts and the chapter rules (`switchAndThreadInstructions`), labelled "Chapter rules (for the planners, not the turns)": since 2026-09-28 no production turn reads them (before, the switch turn did), and they are shown nowhere else.
    - Only the last two start folded (`startsOpen`).
    - Re-rendered on 2026-09-28 with `--rerender-page` from their keys: `turns-r2.html` and the older `round1-turns.html`, `round2-turns.html` and `round3-turns.html` (the pages before are `rating/superseded/*-before-chapter-text.html`). Page ids, item ids and every rating input are unchanged (the inputs' item, label and field tuples hash the same), `turns-r2.html` is byte-identical outside the context, and every context is the old one plus the chapter-text folds and the relabelled chapter rules (the older pages also gain the backfilled chapter question and plan lines of 2026-09-27 and two later rendering fixes outside the context: the pairwise controls' style and script, and an ending's options and interludes left out as the game shows it). `turns-r1.html` was not re-rendered: the reruns rebuild it with new outputs. A field the state lacks shows nothing; an outcome id the story does not hold (a chapter's or one a direction names in parentheses) is named as such. `metadataLeaks` reads every context label as well as the headings. The stylesheet, script and attributes carry none of the blinding words either (CSS's own `none` aside; a test holds it), so a grep audit of a page hits only story text.
    - Re-rendered on 2026-09-29 for the mechanics rows, from their keys: `turns-r1b.html`, `turns-r2.html`, `turns-groups.html` and the three older round pages (the pages before are `rating/superseded/*-before-mechanics.html`). On each, the page data, the item ids and every input's name, value, item, label and field are identical, and the page is byte-identical to the old one once the new `mechanics` and `changes` rows are taken out (the stylesheet and script unchanged), so ratings already saved in a browser still apply.
    - Re-rendered again on 2026-09-29 after the beat repair's bonus ids (`bonusStatIdSeatForm`): `turns-r1b.html` (35 lines), `turns-r2.html` (8), `round1-turns.html` (16), `round2-turns.html` (16) and `round3-turns.html` (12) changed, `turns-groups.html` came out byte-identical (the pages before are `rating/superseded/*-before-bonus-ids.html`). The page data, every input tag and the item ids are identical, and the only segments that differ are stat-bonus lines, each a doubled seat form shown by its id before and by its stat's name now. The same repair reads in `results.md` as `repair:bonusStatIdSeatForm` (and `note:bonusStatUnknown`), which lowers the GPT-6 turn arms' `noRepairs`; B6's balance simulation reads the same on both of its arms.
  - `--pairwise` (the round pages, 2026-09-27): exactly two arms, the reference first and the candidate second, two options per item in balanced random order (the reference sits at A on half the items, by stratum), nine setup items or 14 turn items by default. Each option keeps Acceptable? and a note; ranks give way to one "Which is better?" per item (A, B or About the same). The instructions print the owner's criteria (`PAIRWISE_CRITERIA` in `ratingSets.ts`): for setups, stats that matter in play and keep score sensibly, a real arc for each player, shared outcomes only where they fit, thread types and switch rules that steer; for turns, whether the turn moves the chapter's question and follows its plan, whether the chapter pursues the direction the player picked, whether there is a real choice among the options, and whether the prose sounds like this story. The control (the reference's sample 1 against its sample 2) stays, and the repeat shows the first item's pair with its labels swapped. The layout and the setup and turn context are the ranked page's. The key carries `mode: "pairwise"`, and the export adds `preferences` (item → label or `same`).
  - `--chain-arms <reference chain>,<candidate chain> [--chain-items 4]` (pairwise turn pages, turn round 1): chapter openings beside the regular items. Each is a chapter plan's case; each option is a chain's turn after its plan, with the plan it was written from in a row of its own ("The chapter this version planned": title, kind and type, players or sides, the outcome it advances, length, steps with the first current, possible results; only what every planner form writes, so the row gives no version away). The context stops at the switch the player chose from. The key names each chain item's own `reference`, which `scorePairwise` reads; the reference chain sits at A on every other chain item; the repeat is always a regular item. `keyTokens` scans for both sides of a chain key.
  - A turn item's context shows the chapter's backfilled question and plan ("Chapter question", "Chapter plan") after the kind of milestone, the same for both options. `--frames nearer` (turns-r1b, recorded in the key as `frames`, so a re-render keeps it) shows the nearer frames instead, with their own kind of milestone in place of the stored one.
  - `--chain-cases a,b,…` takes the chapter-opening items from those chapter-planning cases only (turns-r1b took the four of the page it replaced).
  - `--no-repeat` leaves the repeated item out on any page; it is on by default.
  - `--criteria turn-round2` prints turn round 2's questions on a pairwise turn page instead of `PAIRWISE_CRITERIA` (`PAIRWISE_CRITERIA_SETS`); the key keeps it, so a re-render does too. `--criteria groups` prints the group round's (the shared moments told alike, the chapter's question and plan, a real choice that works whatever the others choose, the prose); each option of a group item is every player's turn of one reply. `--criteria options` (2026-09-30) prints the options and continuity page's: options that differ in what you do and which stat or approach they lean on, not only in risk; sacrifices and rewards where they fit the chapter, a second sacrifice with a clear reason in the scene; picking up where the last turn ended and moving the story forward; the chapter's question and plan; the prose.
  - An ending's option shows no options and no interludes, as the game shows it (turn doc M21).
- `--results`: rewrites `results.md` from the stored records and outputs; no API calls (after a reading changes, as the stop rule's did on 2026-09-27).
- `--setup-chain [--cases <chain ids>] [--samples N] [--max-spend 0.20]`: setup round 3's setup-to-play chain (`setupChain.ts`, the mode in `setupChainMode.ts`). Each of the four chain premises (`SETUP_CHAIN_PREMISES`: a 10-turn single-player story, the kids animal rescue, the two-player bounty hunters, the three-player co-founders) gets a new setup on `gpt-6-luna@low/setupR3`, then plays as the game does: the story the setup starts (`storyFromSetup`, a copy of `AIStoryGenerator.createInitialState`'s state; no images, no pregeneration), characters and choices by hash, the game's own dice, each plan checked and applied (`storyAfterAnalysis`), each turn repaired and its changes applied, through the first chapter to the switch turn after it (planners `gpt-6-luna@low/planV2b`; turns `gpt-6-luna@medium/turnB6` for one player, `gpt-6-luna@low/prod` for groups). Before the switch plan after the chapter, a trigger probe asks the planner once more on a copy of the state with the stat of the first switch rule that names a numeric threshold set just across it (`triggerEdit`). Calls are prep jobs (`chain>…`) in `prep-calls.jsonl`, stage setup-rounds; a finished call on the same request is reused, a changed one refused (the dice fall anew: take a new `--samples`). Writes `setup-chain.md` (per chain: each step's wait, cost and checks, the setup's outcomes, stats, rules and thread types, the plans, the turns with options and choices, the probe, stats before and after) and `setup-chain.json` (each step's reply as the game kept it, the states at start and end), keeping every run the file already holds (a chain and sample played again replaces its run). `--report-only` renders the file afresh without calls; `--merge <chain file>` adds another file's runs (a step without its reply in the file gets its output file's reply as written).
- `--playthroughs [--cases <story ids>] [--samples N] [--turns N] [--max-spend 0.70] [--report-only]` (2026-09-30, the coordinator's brief after the owner's "do whatever additional tests you think are useful"): whole stories on production's own code and models, set up and played to their ending (`playthroughs.ts` plays, `playthroughChecks.ts` reads, `playthroughPages.ts` renders, `playthroughMode.ts` calls and writes). Four stories (`PLAYTHROUGHS`): `play-lemonade` (frozen premise `setup-learn-lemonade`, one player, 10 turns), `play-avalon` (`setup-custom-avalon`, one player, 25), `play-food-trucks` (the site's suggestion "competing food truck owners…", two players competitive, 25) and `play-space-pirates` ("space pirates with a shared ship but individual treasure quotas…", three players cooperative-competitive, 25); none of the setup chain's premises.
  - **Production's own code where the game has it:** the setup request (`adopted`, production's limits) with the start rule's one retry (`withOneRetry` on `storyStateStartProblem`, as `StoryCreationService`) and `checkStoryStateBackgrounds`; character selection by hash (`selectCharacters`); each turn in `StoryProgressionService.handleProgression`'s order (a copy: it reaches the database): `resolveCurrentThreads`, the planner `analysisBefore` names through production's `checkedSwitchPlan` / `checkedThreadPlan` (the plan check, its repairs and its one retry told the problem, the length rule), the turn through `checkedBeatReply` (the one-paragraph retry, and since 2026-09-30 the retry on a beat without options outside the ending, with production's ending flag), `repairBeatReply`, `beatStep.apply` and `ChangeService`; each choice through `BeatResolutionService.resolveChoice` with the story's difficulty. Every request is production's own (`requestFor("adopted", …)`), on production's settings with no env set (`settingsFor(resolveTextModelConfig({}))`: Luna low setup and planners, Luna medium single-player turns, Luna low group turns). The story a setup starts is `storyFromSetup` (a copy of `AIStoryGenerator.createInitialState`'s state, as the setup chain uses it; since the review of decision A's fixes, 2026-10-01, it keeps the camps the seat roles name, `campsOfSetup`, as production does, so a run's `start` carries them; the stored rounds' runs predate that, and `playthroughReplay.test.ts` adds them from each run's stored setup where a replay reads them). The ending is read off `getCurrentBeatType() === "ending"` (production's `determineNextBeatType` returns "intro" there, and no planner runs). No images, no pregeneration, no content filter.
  - **The player** (`pickOption`, fixed and varied): a sacrifice or reward whenever one is offered, never twice in a row; otherwise the stat-backed option (the largest counted bonus: the first two, each within ±15), the riskiest (a risky option with the lowest points, else the lowest points) and the most sensible (the highest base points) in turn over the challenge picks; exploration choices rotate by position; each seat starts at its own place in both rotations. Every pick is recorded with its rule, its reason and the game's roll.
  - **The dice** are the game's own (`Math.random` in the two resolution services), run on a source seeded by the story, turn and seat (`withSeededDice`, restored after each synchronous call), so a story played again sends the same requests: an interrupted run resumes, a smoke's turns are the full run's first. A call under the same id on another request is refused.
  - **Calls:** prep jobs (`play>…`, and `judge>…` for the judged checks) in `prep-calls.jsonl`, stage `playthroughs`, under `adopted4` (`PLAYTHROUGH_PROMPT_STATE`: production's code since the ending's adoption, unchanged since the runaway replay tagged it); case ids `<story>-s<n>-<nnn>-<kind>` (a retry `…-retry`). The stories play side by side, each call after the one before it, through `budgetedPrepCall` (`setupChainMode.ts`, the setup chain's call generalised): the invocation never spends past the least of `--max-spend`, the stage cap's remainder and the hard cap's, the calls in flight counted; a call not sent says why (`notSent`), and the story stops there.
  - **Past production's dead end (harness only, marked on the pages):** where production's chapter or switch plan is unusable twice, `handleProgression` fails the turn ("Unable to continue the story") and nothing sends it again. `playAndJudge` passes `retryFailedTurns: 2` (`HARNESS_TURN_RETRIES`): up to two more rounds of production's checked planner, case ids `…-again-N` (each round's retry `…-again-N-retry`); the turn records its `failedRounds`. With `repickStuckSwitches`, a group whose chapter still can't be planned gets `coordinatedRepick`: each player whose pick the plan check names moves to the shared direction another player took (a contested one first), recorded as `repicks` on the turn and `repickedTo` on the pick, then the planner runs once more (`…-after-repick`). Found by the run of 2026-09-30: both group stories hit it (below, Known limits).
  - **Readings** (`readStory`, written to `playthroughs.md`): ends on its turn count (the ending at turn T+1); per chapter the length against PACING's allowed lengths, the stage it settles (from `stageOf` at planning), its result, the milestone the turn after it writes and whether it lands on the outcome; each outcome's milestones stage by stage; each switch's late pacing (binding when fewer threads fit than milestones are still needed, the threads that fit by production's `chaptersThatFit` from the turns left, with `toldFit` where the recorded request told the planner another count; `checkSwitchDesign`'s late checks, which read the rule as measured, ÷ 4; the next chapter's outcome); in group stories (since the review of 2026-09-30) every step of a thread with two or more players, each choice with its own option's result against the result the game used, flagging an exploration step where the outcome's owner is in the thread and another result was used (`groupSteps`, `ownerOverridden`), and each player's switch pick against the thread of the chapter after it, every chapter but the first, with the threads the plan check dropped (`switchPicks`); sacrifices and rewards per chapter and seat against the owner's rule (a second reward or sacrifice offered is flagged; a second sacrifice's reason is read by hand); each lever the player took, paid or not on the next turn (`turnMechanics`' lever readings; since the review, levers of several seats on one shared stat whose one change pays fewer of them than took it count the rest as `sharedOnce`); stat changes that don't fit; plan retries, plan and beat repairs, one-paragraph retries (and those whose retry came back short too, `shortTextUsedAsIs`), the retries on a beat without options (`optionsRetries`, since 2026-09-30; both read the recorded problem's words), the runner's re-sends and calls with no usable reply; the turns production could not get past (`stuckTurns`, with the harness's rounds and re-picks); the turn design checks that failed, and the plan design checks (`planCheckFailures`, by the turn each plan was made); the ending's outcome standing (`outcomeStatesAtEnding`) and the judged checks; waits per turn kind (the planner and the turn, every attempt) against `allowanceFor`, and the turns left out of them (`waitsLeftOut`: the stuck turns); cost. The judged checks after each story: `staysWithinStage` on each chapter thread not at its outcome's last stage (the story as its planner saw it), `outcomesToldAsLeft` per player's ending, Luna low, case ids `judge-play-stage-v2-…` / `judge-play-ending-v1-…` (apart from the stage and ending modes' own).
  - **Files:** `playthroughs.json` (every run: the setup, each turn's calls, plan, repaired reply, mechanics, picks, stats and milestones after it, the states at start and end, the judged checks) and `playthroughs.md`, and a page per story plus an index in `stories/` (offline HTML, not committed: the setup, then every turn with chapter headers, switches, each player's text, the options with their mechanics, the pick, its roll and what the turn changes, each chapter's result, the ending without the options an ending sometimes writes (the game doesn't show them), the stats, the readings; a turn production could not get past and a pick the harness changed are flagged). A story and sample played again replaces its run. `--report-only` renders the files afresh without a call. The dry run prints each story's expected calls (a setup, T+1 turns, a switch and a chapter plan per chapter of 3 to 5 turns) and cost from the measured costs of production's own arms, and each round's judged checks (`judgedEstimate`).
  - **Round 2** (`--playthroughs --round 2`, 2026-09-30, after the owner OK'd "a few more dollars to do useful playthroughs"; `PLAYTHROUGH_ROUNDS` in `playthroughMode.ts`): stage `playthroughs-2` ($1.20), prompt state `adopted7` (production's code since the single-player exploration line and the failed turn's resend and notice), `PLAYTHROUGHS_2` (round 1's four premises, then `play-estate-agents`, the site's flexible competitive #4, two players, 25 turns, one prize and no shared bond so the contest gets the competitive slate's most milestones; and `play-kids-mouse`, read-with-kids single-player #1, age 5, 10 turns, merged as the client merges that category, `kids: true`); files `playthroughs-2.json|md` and pages in `stories/round2/` (round 1's stay as they were). Case ids are round 1's shape; the job key's prompt state keeps them apart.
  - **A failed turn is sent as production sends it** (both rounds' code since then; round 1's stored runs keep the harness's `failedRounds`): the whole turn again from the same story (`writeTurn`: planner, turn, repairs, changes), labels from `turnSends(tryAgain)`: "first", the queue's "resend" (`TURN_RESENDS`, now in `retryOnce.ts` for both), then per press of the notice's Try again (round 2 presses once, round 1 none) "try again" and its "try again resend"; case ids take the label as a suffix (`…-chapter-plan-resend`, `…-try-again-retry`). Each failed send is kept on the turn (`failedSends`: its plan, calls and failure), the send that worked in `sentBy`; the wait sums every send. A call a spend limit kept back is never sent again. Past every send, a group chapter plan unusable on the players' picks still gets the harness's `coordinatedRepick` and one more send (`sentBy: "after repick"`, marked). `retryFailedTurns` is gone.
  - **Readings added for round 2:** `resentTurns` (and waits leave out a turn whose resend failed too, where the players saw the notice; a turn the resend saved counts with every send); `ownStats` (each player's own stats start to end, the turns that moved them; since the review of round 2 the line lists a stat that moved and came back apart, "moved and back where they started", since round 1 read start to end); `leversPaid.chargedAgain` (since that review: each kept reply replayed through production's current beat repairs on the state its turn saw, `replayRun`, listing where `leverChargedAgain` now drops a lever charged again on the turn after its payment; a run played with the repair shows none there and lists it in `fixes`; `undefined`, "not read", where the run can't be replayed); `scoreboard` (each contest scoreboard per turn against the contest results the turn follows, recorded as `contestResults` from production's `resultsFollowed`, now exported: toward the winner, the wrong way, held, moved without a winner or with no contest result, and whether `scoreboardDirection` turned it); `fixes` (the turns production's fixes of 2026-09-30 fired: `lastStepRepeated`, `contestOneSided`, `threadKeptOnPick`, `scoreboardDirection`, `sharedLeverRepeated`, and since the review of round 2 `leverChargedAgain`); and two more judged checks after each story, the choice-result stage's calibrated `optionsFollowResults` per player at every exploration step and `resultsFitKind` per chapter plan (`judge-play-options-v1-…`, `judge-play-results-v1-…`, Luna low), in `choices` and on the pages.
  - **Round 3** (`--playthroughs --round 3`, 2026-10-01, the coordinator's brief after the owner's decisions and fixes of that day): stage `playthroughs-3` ($1.00), prompt state `adopted22` (`PLAYTHROUGHS_3_PROMPT_STATE`, a tag no earlier stage used), `PLAYTHROUGHS_3` (round 2's six premises; `play-kids-mouse` also sends the child's age through the read-with-kids setting, `kidAges` {5, 5} beside the same merged premise, as the setup form does since 2026-10-01, passed by `playthroughSetupInput`), one Try again; files `playthroughs-3.json|md` and pages in `stories/round3/` (the index links rounds 1 and 2).
    - **Recording since round 3:** each chapter plan records `pacingDecision.pacedLengths` (production's PACING since the pacing-clues adoption; `planPacing` defaults its lengths to `pacedLengths`), which the chapter readings read before `allowedLengths`; a switch plan's call log records `lengthProblem` from `switchPacingProblem`, `checkedSwitchPlan`'s default.
    - **Readings added for round 3:** a group challenge or contest step on one player's own outcome, the owner in the thread, read against the owner's roll alone (`ownersRoll`, `ownersRollResult`, `ownersRollLine`; the group table's "Owner's roll counted" column, and the pages flag a step that went another way); the lever flags "reward after the chapter's first step" (one player only) and "reward in consecutive chapters" (O2c's placement); the story's pacing as the pacing-clues stage read it (`pacingSummary` over `readLatePacing`, `pacingLine`); a story read with a child, turn by turn against its band (`kidsReadings`, `kidsLine`, "Read with a child" in the md and a line under each kids turn on the pages; the setting's ages, else the premise's line, else 6-8; `kidsReadability.ts`); `milestoneNotPlayed` and `contestInCooperative` among the fixes, whose label is now "production's fixes that fired".
    - **Readings added by the review of round 3** (2026-10-01, no calls; every round re-renders with them): `leversPaid.chargedAgain` now replays production's widened `leverChargedAgain` (a repeat on a later step of the chapter that paid it, and a ladder's step; the line reads "on the next turn or a later step of the same chapter"), and `leversPaid.chargedOnOffer` its note `leverChargedOnOffer` (a lever's stat moved in the reply that offers that lever), from the same replay; `leversPaid.undone` (a lever paid on its turn whose stat a turn or two later moved back, read on the stat values each turn left: a number or a ladder the other way, a list holding a removed item again; a lever the same player took on that stat in between stops it); `ownStats[].reversals` (a number's or a ladder's changes the other way from the change before; the line says so from two, "4 of them the other way from the change before", so a stat that went back and forth no longer reads as plain movement); `resultWords` (the result words a player read as story text, production's `resultWordsOfBeat`, the note `resultWordsInText`'s reading); the scoreboard reading finds a board its outcome names by the stat's id (the food trucks' "Scored by Contract Race" for `shared_contract_race`, which had read "none"), since the decision-A fixes of that evening through production's own `scoreboardOf`, which reads the id and its words too (`contestBoardsOf`; until then the reading's own `boardOf`, which marked such a board "its outcome names it by the stat's id, which production's scoreboard repair doesn't read"), and associates a recorded contest result without a board by its outcome (a run played before then recorded none there); production's `scoreboardWrittenTwice` is among the fixes that fired; `setupChecksFailed` (`checkSetupDesign` read afresh on the stored setup reply, so a check added later reads every round: `distinctIdentities`, which the runs stored but no reading printed, and `moneyIsNumber`); the pages print the paced lengths the .md prints (`pacedLengths` before `allowedLengths`; they had printed the older allowed ones), and the readings section carries the new lines. The harness's setup asks once more on a seat whose identities share a name, as production does (`withDistinctIdentityNames`, the setup's call log records the line as its problem; the readings' "setup asked again" says so).
- `--balance-sim [--arms <beat keys>] [--prompt-state <tag>]`: B6's balance simulation (`balanceSim.ts`), today's stored challenge sets (default `gpt-6-luna@medium/prod` under `round0`) scored with the game's own resolution code as written and on B6's scale, into `balance-sim.md`; no API calls. The final check's reading on production's own options (`--arms gpt-6-luna@medium/adopted --prompt-state adopted1`) is kept beside it as `balance-sim-adopted1.md`, since a run writes over `balance-sim.md`.
- `--rerender-page <pageId>`: renders the page of an existing key afresh from the stored outputs, after a rendering fix. The page id, items and labels stay the same, so ratings a browser has already saved still apply, and the key is not rewritten. It writes `rating/text-<kind>-<pageId>.html`, so rename it over the handed-out file.
- `--score <ratings-export.json>`: scores an export against its answer key, found in `keys/` or else in `keys/superseded/` (`readKey`, since 2026-09-28), so an export from a replaced page (the old `turns-r1.html`) still scores. Ranks and Acceptable? verdicts count independently, so a rank-only export scores in full and its acceptable columns read "not marked". The owner ranks the best and leaves the rest, so on an item with at least one rank the unranked options read as worse than every ranked one and tied with each other: they take the rank one below the worst given there for the mean, lose to any ranked option and tie with each other in wins/ties/losses, and the repeat and control are read the same way. An item with no rank stays unrated. The report lists each item's unranked options ("read as worse, unordered" where the item carries a rank) and every wholly unrated item, and calls the repeat or control reading unavailable when its items have no ratings.
  - A pairwise key scores with `scorePairwise`: a win, tie or loss per item for the candidate, the candidate's share (a tie counts half), a two-sided exact sign test over the decided items, the acceptable rate of each arm and the items acceptable on one side only, the repeat read in arm terms (same result or not), which control sample was preferred, picks by label, a per-item table with the case, and every note.
- `--check-baselines [--ratings <export.json>,…]`: the improvement documents' new automatic checks (below, "Design checks") over every stored output, no API calls. It writes `check-baselines.md` and `.json`: per role and arm each check's pass rate, count's mean and pooled share, with the two-sample noise (the arm's sample 1 against its sample 2 on the cases where it has both); for each rated ranked page given, which checks separate the owner's rank-1 picks (agree: a rank-1 option passes and a lower one fails; against: the reverse); single-player waits per turn kind (chains as one wait, turns and planners alone); how many stored `/prod` requests today's code rebuilds byte for byte; and a hash-ordered sample of stat readouts for reading that check's false alarms by hand.
- `--filter-check [--arms <k1,k2>] [--fresh] [--max-spend <usd>]`: the content filter's fixed test set (`filterCases.ts`, 37 cases) through the production filter path, per arm: `ContentFilterService` with `contentFilterClassifier`, so the premise and image-request prompts, the fail-closed retry and the filter role's retries, timeout and output cap are production's. A recording fetch prices every HTTP attempt, LangChain's retries included.
  - Arms (`FILTER_ARMS` in `filterCheck.ts`): `gpt-4.1-mini@t0.2/prod` (the pre-migration filter), `gpt-6-luna@low/prod` (the default pair) and `gpt-6-luna@medium/prod` (the fallback). Other keys are refused.
  - Cases are allow (gated: refusing one fails), refuse (gated: letting one through fails) or either (borderline, reported only). An arm passes when it refuses every must-refuse case, allows every clearly allowed one and answers every case (an unavailable verdict, the fail-closed error, fails it). Arms are interleaved per case, so a cap stop leaves them comparable.
  - A case and arm pair with a verdict is skipped; an unavailable one is asked again. `--fresh` asks every case again (after a prompt, model or limit change); the latest record per pair counts, and every record stays in the ledger.
  - Writes `filter-check.jsonl` (one record per case and arm: verdict, reason, wait, every attempt's usage, the cap and effort sent, cost) and `filter-check.md` (per arm: the reading, missed refusals, refused allowed cases, waits, output tokens, replies cut at the cap, spend; then every case's verdicts), covering every arm with records.
  - Run of 2026-09-27: both default arms passed; Luna low was re-run fresh under the final 15 s and 2,000-token limits with the same verdicts; $0.013 in all (`.context/content-safety.md`).
- The turn rounds' preparation (`turnPrep.ts`; turn doc section 4, "Before round 1", items 4 to 6), all in the `turn-rounds` stage and under `--max-spend` (default $0.10). The dry run prints what each would still send.
  - `--build-round-cases [--rebuild-cases]`: freezes the cases play never produced (`roundCases.ts`, below under Cases) beside the others. The few turns and plans no stored reply covers are written by production's GPT-6 default for the role (`productionArm`), recorded in `calls.jsonl`; a call on a round case's own input is recorded under that case's id at sample 1, so the migration check reuses it. A finished call under an id is reused only on the same prompt; otherwise the build stops, since the build's dice set every later state.
  - `--backfill-chapters`: a chapter question and plan per stored chapter (`chapterFrames.ts`, below under Checks), one Luna low call each, into `prep-calls.jsonl`, then `chapter-frames.json`. `--frames nearer --stage plan-refresh` is the nearer backfill (below, "Chapter frames"), into `chapter-frames-nearer.json`; `--cases` sends only the chapters those cases read (a smoke), and the file is still written over every chapter.
  - `--judge-calibration [--arms gpt-6-luna@low|gpt-6-luna@medium] [--samples 2]`: the judged checks (`judgedChecks.ts`) on the hand-read turns, into `prep-calls.jsonl`, then `judge-calibration.md` and `.json` over every judge and prompt version with records.
  - `--judge-records --arms <beat or chain keys> --prompt-state <tag> [--samples 1]`: the judged checks on a round's turns after the first (`roundTurnsToJudge`: every final usable beat record of the arms, isolated or a chain's turn on its own plan, one judge call per player on Luna low, prompt v3; chapter steps get all three checks, switch turns and endings the first paragraph, since turn round 2; new calls go only to samples 1 and 2 and, with `--cases`, to those cases, while every turn already judged is still read), into `prep-calls.jsonl`, then `judged-turns.md` and `.json`: each arm's pass rates, and a candidate against its reference (`referenceKeyOf`) on the matched turns with the reference's two-sample noise and the stop rule. The judge reads the chapter's own question and plan (planner v2) before a backfilled frame. `--frames nearer --stage reruns` judges on the nearer frames: a turn whose judge request they change gets its own call (`judge-v3-nearer-…`), the reference's stored turns included, so both sides of a comparison read the same frame; a turn they leave unchanged (a chain's own plan, a switch turn) reuses its call; the readings go to `judged-turns-nearer.md` and `.json` beside the rounds' file.
  - `--judge-groups --arms <reference,candidates…> --prompt-state <tag> [--stage groups]`: the group round's judged check (`groupJudge.ts`), one Luna low call per group reply (every player's turn as that player reads it, who shares which switch or chapter): `sharedMomentsMatch` (the moments the players share are told alike: the same people, events and lines, each by the same speaker in the same words; yes when they share none) and `sharedFactsAgree` (no turn contradicts another on who is where, what is there, what happened before). Calibration (`GROUP_JUDGE_CALIBRATION`): today's 12 stored group turns (the reference's sample 1, judged twice), read by hand in the owner's feedback workflow on the same criterion (shared moments 3 yes, 6 no, 3 partial where one conversation is paraphrased; facts 12 yes). Every group reply of the arms (samples 1 and 2) is judged once; a candidate reads against its reference with the reference's two-sample noise and the stop rule. Writes `judged-groups.md` and `.json`; books to the stage given (default `groups`). Run of 2026-09-28: neither check reliable by `isReliable`: shared moments agree 7 of 9 (hand yes 1 of 3, hand no 6 of 6, samples 11 of 12), and on a re-read both of its false fails are defensible under the question's own wording (a notebook open in one turn and shut in the other; a line one turn leaves out where its character is present), so it reads stricter than the hand and never passed a turn the hand failed: a lower bound. Facts have no hand no.
  - `--build-stage-cases [--rebuild-cases]` (`stagePrep.ts`, `stageCases.ts`, 2026-09-29, no calls; cases already frozen are left as they are unless `--rebuild-cases`, `stageCasesToFreeze`): freezes the climax arm's three last chapters (2026-09-30, `LAST_CHAPTER_CASES`, category `last-chapter`: `round-thread-last4-8988006e-t5` and `round-thread-last2-8988006e-t5`, Novi Reg's second chapter plan after the switch's first and second option with `turnsLeft(4)` and `turnsLeft(2)`, the Waste Ring at 1 of 3; `round-thread-last4-mp-965413e1-p3-t5`, the three-player second chapter plan with `turnsLeft(4)`, three outcomes at 0 of 2; no stored planning case is in its story's last thread with an outcome needing several milestones) and `round-thread-first-8988006e-t1` beside the round cases (source `round`, category `first-chapter`), the Arielle story (Novi Reg) before its first chapter, the chapter the owner's feedback read, which no stored case held: `cont-8988006e-t2-o0`'s state cut back to the chapter's start as the chapter backfill cuts it (`chapterStart`, `stateAtChapterStart`; its stats, facts and elements one turn later, so one Waste Ring fact from the chapter's first step shows). `addCases` without a report leaves `round-build-report.json` as it was. Older stages that plan every thread case now show two open jobs on it in the dry run (turn round 1's `planV2`, the final check's `adopted`); the stage scoping runs `planV2c` and today's form on it, whose keys the plan refresh and the migration check share.
  - `--judge-stages [--arms <chapter planner keys>] --prompt-state <tag> [--cases <item or case ids>]` (`stagePrep.ts`, `stageJudge.ts`, 2026-09-29): the judged check `staysWithinStage`, one Luna low call per chapter thread whose outcome has a later stage (stage k of n with k < n; none at the last stage or once complete). The judge reads the outcome (question, endings, intended milestones, milestones so far, the stage the chapter settles) and what every planner form writes (title, players, the chapter question where one is written, a kind of milestone that is not the question, every step with its results, the last one pointing at the milestones where it repeats them, the possible milestones), never a plan's own stage list or plan text; it names the stages itself first, then evidence, then yes or no; its example is another story's (Mara's baking championship), not the planner's. Plans are read after the game's plan check; a plan passes when every applicable thread does. Calibration (`STAGE_JUDGE_CALIBRATION`, 24 hand-read plans at two samples: 10 no, the owner's Waste Ring chapter and its twin from the other Novi Reg playthrough, six stored gpt-4.1-mini plans on today's form and two constructed failing versions of planner v2c plans; 11 yes, today's form on Luna and gpt-4.1-mini, planner v2 and v2c, a stored chapter; 3 partial, left out of agreement), reliable by `isReliable`. Every stored chapter the cases read (`chaptersToJudge`, the pseudo-arm "stored chapters (in play)") and every final usable isolated plan of the arms (one sample; the calibration's sample 1 is also its plan's reading) are judged; a candidate reads against its reference and second references on the (case, sample) pairs both have, with the reference's two-sample noise and the stop rule (`stageReadings`). Books to `stage-scoping`; writes `judged-stages.md` and `.json`. Estimates err high (1,000 output tokens a call; measured about $0.0003 a call), so judging five arms needs `--max-spend 0.15`.
    - Prompt v2 (`STAGE_JUDGE_PROMPT_VERSION`, 2026-09-30) adds `STAGE_DECISION_RULE`, the calibration's one fix: decide by what each step asks and what each result says has happened, not by how the judge worded the stages; a step asking how a character carries out a later stage's task reaches past it whatever its results; a result reaches past only when it records a later stage's result, or the outcome's, as done; one that makes this stage's result bigger or a later stage easier stays within, "however big its words" (with a Mara example). v1 agreed with the hand on 20 of 21 (hand yes 10 of 11, no 10 of 10) but its samples on only 20 of 24: each flip rode on how broadly it named a later stage that time ("significantly boosting their fame" read as the next stage's), or let a step asking how to expose the Ring pass on its results. v2: 21 of 21 (11 of 11, 10 of 10), samples 23 of 24, reliable. The one item its samples still split on is the twin, the owner's own failure mode: sample 2 names stage 1 "Expose the Waste Ring" and passes the "attempt to expose" step because no result dismantles anything. So a plan reading, one sample each, can still pass such a step now and then; the owner's first chapter is read by hand as well. v1's records stay under their own keys.

  - `--judge-clues [--cases <item or case ids>]` (`latePacingPrep.ts`, `cluesJudge.ts`, 2026-10-01): two judged checks, Luna low, into `prep-calls.jsonl` under `adopted15`, booked to `late-pacing`, then `judged-clues.md` and `.json`: `noNewMystery` on each player's turn in the story's late part (no unexplained detail the facts and the player's earlier interludes don't hold, in the text, interludes or recorded facts) and `detailsExplained` on the ending (at least one of the story's unexplained details explained). The judge reads every recorded fact, the player's earlier interludes and the turn; never the prompt. Prompt v1 (`CLUES_JUDGE_PROMPT_VERSION`). Calibrated on 36 items read by hand before any judge call (`CLUES_CALIBRATION`: 25 late turns of the second round, 14 yes, 7 no, 4 partial; the six stored endings, no, and five constructed versions each explaining one detail, yes; one item first read as new, the food trucks' turn 23 notches, corrected to yes on a second reading before any call: a fact since turn 14), two samples: **neither reliable**. `noNewMystery` 18 of 21 (yes 11 of 14, no 7 of 7), samples 23 of 25: its false fails read a new facet of an earlier detail (a hum beneath the familiar chime, the paper lifting with a knock, the taps "maybe a signal") or the food trucks' shifting route map as new. `detailsExplained` 10 of 11 (yes 5 of 5, no 5 of 6), samples 11 of 11: it reads the mouse ending's "sending a signal" as an explanation. Not run on the stage's replies (the cap): the stage's verdict on clues rests on the hand reading. This mode keeps prompt v1 (`LATE_PACING_CLUES_VERSION`; its requests rebuild byte for byte as sent, a test against `prep-calls.jsonl`); v2 runs in the pacing-clues stage (below).

**Budget** (owner, 2026-09-26).
- The target was about $25 overall; the hard cap is $48 (`HARD_CEILING` in `budget.ts`), raised from $30 to $33 by the owner on 2026-09-27 for the rounds after the Round 0 play fixes, to $40 on 2026-09-28 for the missing steps after the owner's feedback of that day (the owner went on although the stalled Stage 4 calls may have been billed: `UNRECORDED_STAGE4_USD`, about $1.3, which the ledger does not hold and the dry run's total line names), and to $42 on 2026-09-30 for a second round of whole-story playthroughs on production's current code (the owner OK'd "a few more dollars to do useful playthroughs"; the coordinator set $42, with the ledger at $37.24 and $2.76 left, about $1.46 with the stalled Stage 4 calls on top), and to $45 on 2026-10-01 for the fixes the owner decided that day and the measurements they need (the owner: "do all the fixes and the measurements needed for them", "few bucks don't matter. I just want to make sure that we stay frugal and only run what's needed"; the ledger at $39.94 and $2.06 left, about $0.76 with the stalled Stage 4 calls on top; every paid run still gets its own stage, cap and reason), and to $48 on the evening of 2026-10-01 for round 3's open defects (the status note's decision A, which the coordinator settled as everything: the no-call fixes, both group changes measured, the seal, the money setup rule and turn line, the result-words line and a full confirming round, about $2 beyond what the $45 left; under the owner's standing "do all the fixes and the measurements needed for them", "few bucks don't matter. I just want to make sure that we stay frugal and only run what's needed" and "no need to be more frugal than before"; the ledger at $43.61, `LEDGER_WHEN_DECISION_A_OPENED`, and $1.39 left, about $0.09 with the stalled Stage 4 calls on top; every paid run still gets its own stage, cap and reason, a smoke first and a dry-run count to compare).
- The eval's price table (`pricing.ts`) carries `gpt-6.1-sol` since 2026-09-30 at OpenAI's rates ($2 input, $0.10 cached input, $2.50 cache write, $10 output per 1M tokens): today's Sol's but for cached input. Its reasoning-token and speed estimates read as Sol's until it has measurements. No arm, stage or probe check runs it yet.
  - Going a little over $25 is justified because the owner explicitly prioritised Sol for story setups.
  - Setup inputs are also 21–23K tokens with the schema, not the 15K the test plan assumed.
- Stage caps: Stage 0 (probe, case building, both baselines) $8, Stages 1–2 $13, Stage 3 $3, Stage 4 $4 (closed).
- The rounds after the Round 0 play fixes (2026-09-27) have their own ledger stages inside the then $33 hard cap, each cap with its reason in `STAGE_CAP_REASONS` (`budget.ts`; the dry run prints it beside the spend). They opened at $26.39 spent (`LEDGER_WHEN_ROUNDS_OPENED`), and $26.39 + $3 + $2 + $1.20 + the filter check's $0.30 is $32.89.
  - `setup-rounds`, $3.00 (coordinator): setup rounds 1 to 3 on Luna low, with Sol low in round 1 (about $0.95 for nine premises) and a two-sample Luna noise run per round (about $0.11). Round 1's dry run (2026-09-27): 45 jobs, est $0.93 (Luna low 36, $0.18; Sol low 9, $0.75); by hand from the stored production setups on the same cases (Luna $0.005-0.007, Sol $0.090-0.121 each, round 1's input about 2K tokens smaller) about $0.21-0.24 and $0.90-1.00, so about $1.2. Round 2's dry run: 72 jobs, est $0.36; by hand about $0.43-0.47 (its input is about production's size again, since production's example stat setups are back); it cost $0.43.
