- The filter check is its own ledger stage, `filter` (`LEDGER_STAGES` in `budget.ts`), capped at $0.30: it has no `--run` stage, and its calls cost fractions of a cent. Its spend is read from `filter-check.jsonl` and counts toward the global cap; the dry run and `results.md` list it beside the stages. Before a run it is priced at one answer per open case and arm, a GPT-6 answer at the role's output cap, so the estimate errs high.
- A stage cap above its default (`--stage-cap`) needs `--over-target-reason "<why>"`, which is appended to `budget-overrides.jsonl`. On `--run` it also needs `--arms`: a raise is approved for arms, so the run refuses to start without them, and the override records them. Plan order cannot hold a raise to the approved arms, because the runner runs every setup job before any beat job and warm-first can start a later arm early. `--global-cap` can only lower the $48; no flag or reason raises it.
- `--max-spend` caps one invocation. A mode that makes several runner passes (the case builds, `--judge-calibration` over several judges) takes what the earlier passes spent off the next one's allowance (`capsAfter` in `turnPrep.ts`); `--judge-calibration` also refuses on every judge's estimate together, before it sends anything.
- The runner reserves each call's estimate, re-sends included, before it starts, so parallel calls overshoot a cap only by the estimates' error. Setup estimates read low (see Estimates), so with 6 calls in flight a setup invocation can pass a cap by up to about $0.1.
- Groups run setup first and pipeline chains last. A cap stop cuts from the end of that order, so the chains go first and Sol setup is reached last.
- The owner's shrink order when a dry run predicts an overrun:
  1. `--no-mp-continuations`;
  2. analysis at one sample (`--role analysis --samples 1`);
  3. never Sol setup.
- Probe spend counts against Stage 0, including earlier probe runs (`priorSpendUsd`).

**Runner.**
- Transport failures (429, 5xx, timeouts, dropped connections) retry after a backoff, on their own budget of 3. A 429 whose code is `credit_balance_exhausted` or `insufficient_quota` is never retried and leaves its job open (`NEVER_RETRY_CODES`, `finishesJob`, since 2026-09-29): it says nothing about the model, and the next invocation sends it once the account has credit.
- A reply production's LangChain parse rejects is re-sent at once, at most `PRODUCTION_MAX_RETRIES` (2) times, as production does. That covers `repaired` (text after the JSON), `invalid-json`, `schema-mismatch`, `length` and `refusal`.
- A 400 is never retried. One naming a parameter (`rejectedParam`) leaves its job open: the next invocation plans it again. `finishesJob` in `runner.ts` is the one definition of finished, used for resuming, for case building (`finishingRecord`) and for the variant section's pairs. Validity, the call count, the cost and input-cost means and the cache readings leave such attempts out, like transport failures, and the rejected-parameter rate still reads over all records. So a request-shape bug found by a smoke run leaves no dead job, and a job still waiting for its re-run is neither a matched pair nor a $0 call.
- **Production's one checked retry of a turn** (`CheckedRetry` in `runner.ts`, `checkedRetry` in `jobPlan.ts`, since 2026-09-30): a turn job of a stage `stageChecksTurns` names (only `choice-line-sp`) carries production's check and retry (`checkedBeatReply`, `beatChecks.ts`). Where the first reply as parsed is one short paragraph or has no options (`beatReplyProblem`, with the ending's flag), the runner sends the retry right after it as the job's step 2, the first request told the problem (`withBeatProblem`), on the same arm, schema and limits, with production's parse re-sends inside it. The first reply's final record then carries `checkedRetry` (`short`, `noOptions` or `both`) and does not end the job; the retry's records carry it too, and its final record ends the job. A job whose retry is due but unrecorded (an interrupted run) is open, and sends step 1 again on the next invocation. The generic readings read a turn by its first reply: `isResultRecord` and the turn waits leave a turn's step 2 out (`isCheckedRetry`), a first reply the retry follows is a finished pair (`finishesFirstReply`), and an arm's prose counts it once; the reply the game keeps, the wait including the retry and the cost are the checked-turn report's (`--choice-line-sp`, above).
- Every attempt is a record in `calls.jsonl`. Attempts are numbered after those already recorded for the job and step, so a re-run (a re-planned rejected request, or a job resumed part-way) gets new callIds and never overwrites an earlier attempt's `outputs/<callId>.json`. A job without records starts at attempt 1.
- **Each job is planned once** (`oncePerKey` in `planJobs`, since 2026-09-29): two plans of one arm on one case meet where their samples overlap, which `--samples` causes by raising every plan's count (the second Casablanca retest's `--samples 6` raised round 3's two-sample plan beside the retest's samples 3 to 6). Before the fix both copies ran at once under one callId, and the second reply overwrote the first's output file. It happened once: `setupR3` samples 3 to 6 on Casablanca, 2026-09-28 23:39-23:41 UTC, eight records for four jobs, $0.025 for the four lost replies. Those records stay in `calls.jsonl`, since both copies were billed. The readings in `results.md` take one record per job attempt (`oneRecordPerAttempt` in `runner.ts`: the last written, whose reply the file holds, checked on all four pairs), and spend counts every record. No other attempt in the ledger is recorded twice. Other readers (`--check-baselines`, the rating pages) still see both copies of those four jobs, with the one reply they share.
- **Split requests** (Stage 4) go out per model family (`chatInput` in `executor.ts`):
  - gpt-6: a `developer` message whose text part carries `prompt_cache_breakpoint: { mode: "explicit" }` (the factory already sends explicit cache mode), then the user message;
  - gpt-4.x: a plain `system` message, then the user message. Its implicit prefix cache needs no breakpoint, and the field is gpt-5.6+ only.
  - The factory is unchanged and no TTL is set. The stored prompt holds both parts around `MESSAGE_SEPARATOR`.
- **Warm-first scheduling.** A split request's job carries a cache line: the first 12 characters of `sha256(arm key | JSON schema | fixed)`, copied onto every record of the job.
  - The first call of a line runs alone. The rest of the line starts once it has finished, whatever its outcome. Other lines, and jobs without a line, are never held.
  - A worker left with only held jobs waits for a warm-up to finish, then checks for a stop again.
  - Cases queue in turn order: story, then turn; setup by player count. GPT-6's fixed block is story-independent, so this mainly helps gpt-4.1-mini's implicit cache of a story's state prefix.

**Arms.** They are hard-coded in `arms.ts`, the baseline included. `baselineArm` returns `COMPARISON_BASELINE`: production's settings before the GPT-6 migration (gpt-4.1 for setup and the template editor, gpt-4.1-mini for turns and analysis, temperature 0.2, single- and multiplayer alike), which every stored baseline record carries. Until 2026-09-27 it followed production config; production now refuses gpt-4.x, so the eval reads no production env and keeps comparing against the stored gpt-4.1 records. Where a report says "today's production" or "today's model", it means this comparison, not the GPT-6 defaults.
- Arm key format: `<model>@<effort | t<temperature>>[+v<verbosity>]/<variant>`, for example `gpt-6-luna@medium/prod` or `gpt-4.1-mini@t0.2/prod`. A chain's key is `pipeline:<analysis key>><beat key>`. `arms.ts` owns both formats (`armKey`, `referenceKey`, `chainKey`, `chainSides`), so build and parse keys only through them.
- Variants (`variants.ts`): `prod` is what production sent at the `round0` prompt state (`storyTextRound0/`, frozen at the adoption of 2026-09-28; every report's "today's form"), and Stage 3's "full" form. `adopted` is what production sends now (`storyTextSteps.ts`, and `TemplateService.iterateTemplate`'s request for AI Iteration), with production's timeout and output cap for the role and player count (`productionCallLimits`, as `limits`, since 2026-09-28), read against `prod`; the form gate and the paid final check run it under `adopted1` (records under `round0` would name the code before the adoption). `adoptedTemplate` (setup only, the final check) is production's template generation, the template editor's AI Draft: `setupStep`'s template kind (3-5 difficulty levels and a teaser, no kids budget: templates have no category) with the template editor's limits (`productionCallLimits`: 20,000 tokens, and 240 s since 2026-09-29; the final check ran it at 150 s), read against Sol low's stored custom-story setups on today's form, since no template form had run; `checkSetup`'s `difficultyModifier` reads a template's three to five levels, each on an allowed modifier, where a custom story needs its one. `slim` (beats only) drops the stats list, the multiplayer coordination note and six plan strings; `showDontTell` stays, and the options check keeps `previousOptionsToAvoid` and `upToOneSacrificeOrRewardOption`. `minimal` (setup, beats, switch, thread) drops every field test plan §5 lets Stage 3 drop. Every rule a dropped line carried stays as one sentence, so Stage 3 measures "written plan versus private plan", not "fewer rules".
  - Stage 4's split requests (`storyTextRewrite/`): `rewrite` is beats on production's full planning fields and setup with production's example stat setups; `rewriteSlim` is beats on the slim fields; `rewriteZeroShot` is setup without the examples. These build the `exact` counts. `rewrite2`, `rewrite2Slim` and `rewrite2ZeroShot` build the same with `worded` counts (the count fix). A variant throws on a role it does not cover.
  - The setup rounds' candidates (`storyTextRounds/`): `setupR1` is setup round 1 for custom-story setup and AI Iteration. Against production it: puts "How the game plays your setup" (the engine facts, checked against today's code) after the opening line; replaces the outcome budget with a fixed Outcomes section and a per-call "This setup" slate after the Game mode line (one player: no shared list in the schema, three own outcomes; multiplayer: one or two shared, a personal floor, a fixed role per seat in every multiplayer mode, premise names allowed); prints a scoreboard rule and the contested-favor rule in competitive and cooperative-competitive games only; swaps the don't-use-stats lines for "stats that act in play"; replaces the two example stat setups, the long type catalogue, the old engine paragraph and the "For each stat" block with a one-line catalogue and one goblin worked example; rewrites the outcome, resolution, resonance and stat descriptions (±15 scale, certain sacrifices, milestone counts 1 to 3 in generation); words the can-change-in-beats flag as the beats' stat view labels it, apart from sacrifices and rewards; and adds the no-blank-items sentence. The scoreboard exceptions, the resonance's shared and contested lines and the rules about two players' outcomes print only where the call has them. In AI Iteration, A1.6's one-list line prints for every player count, because a single-player template made before round 1 can hold its outcomes in the shared list. Caps only: shared outcomes 2 (multiplayer), a seat's outcomes 3, effects 3, adjustments 2; no `minItems`.
  - `setupR2` and `setupR2Order` are setup round 2's arms A and B (`storyTextRounds/setupRound2.ts`), custom-story setup and AI Iteration. Caps only: thread types 8, instructions 4, narrative implications 3, effects 3, adjustments 2. A request with `assemble` (arm B) has its parsed reply stored as the assembled fields (`assembledReply` in `variants.ts`, applied by the executor), so every reader, the checks and the rating page included, sees today's shape; the raw body keeps the reply as written.
  - `setupR1b` is round 1 with the round-1 report's one-sentence fixes (`ROUND1B_PARTS`, `fixes`): the scoreboard's tooltip (two players) and leader values (three players) name no seat, since each seat's backgrounds carry its role; a two-player contest's scoreboard has one or two effects, its catch-up among them (the effects field, contest modes only); the worked example's "Energy" is "Fervor"; and the one-line catalogue's closing "In general, favor string and string[] …" goes (the review read: the stat guidelines' first bullet says it). `setupR1c` is round 1b with proposal 1's one fix-and-retest (`ROUND1C_PARTS`, `everyPlayerStat`): in multiplayer with the seat roles, "Every player gets every player stat, so none is written for one role or named after one player." Round 1's own requests stay byte for byte as they ran.
  - Turn round 1 (`storyTextRounds/turnRound1*.ts`): `planV2` (switch and thread analysis: A2 to A6 with binding late pacing, the reply assembled into today's plan) and `planV2Full` (the same with the restated switch/thread instructions kept, A5's trigger comparison); `chapterFull` and `chapterSlim` (a chapter step's beat with B2 and B3 rows 11, 14 and 15, on production's reply or B4's slim one; chapter steps only, slim single-player only); `chapterSlimPlans` (slim's one fix-and-retest: slim with production's `beatTypeConsiderations` and `worldBuilding` planning fields back, in production's places). References: `planV2`, `chapterFull`, `chapterSlim` and `chapterSlimPlans` read against `prod` (the migration check's `round0` records), `planV2Full` against `planV2`; second references `chapterSlim` against `chapterFull`, `chapterSlimPlans` against `chapterSlim` and `chapterFull`, and `planV2Full` against `prod`. A beat request carries its case's backfilled chapter frames (`requestInputFor`), which only these variants read.
  - Turn round 2 (`storyTextRounds/turnRound2.ts`): `turnR2b` (every single-player turn), `turnR2Paragraphs` (the same with B9 item 2) and `turnR2c` (B5's one fix-and-retest: the last paragraph at the others' length, no option list in a character's words, the hooks rule rationing mysteries, not facts). `turnR2` is the name the smoke ran the draft under (five turns, its first-turn text fixed after it); it is not planned again. References: `turnR2`, `turnR2b` and `turnR2c` against `prod`; `turnR2Paragraphs` against `turnR2b` with `prod` as its second reference; `turnR2c` with `turnR2b` as its second.
  - Turn round 3 (`storyTextRounds/turnRound3.ts`): `turnR3Form` (B9, every single-player turn, split, production's limits), against `turnR2b` with `prod` as its second reference; priced from `turnR2b` until it has measured outputs.

The ending (2026-09-30): `endingState` against `adopted` on the same turn model (Luna medium for one player, Luna low for groups), under `adopted2`.   - **Noise from the arm a reference builds on** (`noiseReferenceKey` in `arms.ts`, `variantComparisons`): where a reference ran once, its noise is the two samples of the arm it builds on, on the same cases, in the arm's state or stored and rebuilt byte for byte; the row says "one sample; noise from …".   - **Stand-ins** (`standInKey` in `arms.ts`): where a reference arm has no record on one of the candidate's pairs, a stand-in arm's record counts as the reference's when today's code builds the reference variant's request for that case byte for byte the same, prompt hash and JSON schema (`sameRequestAs` in `jobPlan.ts`, passed to `variantComparisons` by `results.md`). Only planner v2's for planner v2b: v2b's chapter requests are v2's for one player (two-sided contests change group prompts only), and v2b ran alone only in groups (the plan refresh). Otherwise it is the variant's base (`VARIANT_REFERENCE`): slim, minimal, rewrite, rewrite2 and setupR1 → prod; rewriteSlim and rewrite2Slim → slim; rewriteZeroShot → rewrite; rewrite2ZeroShot → rewrite2; setupR2 → setupR1 (round 2 against round 1) and setupR2Order → setupR2 (the order on its own); setupR1b and setupR1c → prod; setupR2b and setupR2bOrder → setupR1c (their base); prod has none. The baseline can be a reference: `gpt-4.1-mini@t0.2/rewrite` reads against today's `gpt-4.1-mini@t0.2/prod`. Estimates walk `estimateBaseKey` instead, which puts a count-fix arm's Stage 4 form (`EARLIER_FORM`) before its reference.

- Stage 1–2 (owner decisions, 2026-09-26):
  - Setup: Sol low ×2, medium ×1, none ×1; Luna none, low and medium ×2.
  - Beats: Luna medium, none and low on all cases ×2; Luna high on the 15-case subset ×2; Sol low on the subset ×1.
  - Analysis: Luna none, low and medium ×2.
  - Pipeline: Luna low analysis, then each Luna beat arm.
  - Rare-failure batch: 50 extra single-sample Luna beat calls per effort.
- Stage 3 (Milestone 3). This is the coordinator's carry-forward, chosen before the owner rated Round 1. The full forms are the Stage 1–2 `prod` records and are not re-run. So Stage 3 ran with `--prompt-state postfix`: the comparison pairs arms only within one prompt state. A Stage 3 run under a new tag therefore needs its full forms (the Stage 1–2 `prod` arms) recorded under that tag too.
  - Setup: Sol low minimal on 9 premises (`STAGE3_SETUP_PREMISES`: 3 per player count, every game mode, a Kids premise and three dark ones) ×1. Luna low minimal on all 18 ×2.
  - Beats: Luna medium and Luna low, each slim and minimal, and a Luna none minimal control, on single-player cases ×2. The control comes last, so a cap stop cuts it first.
  - Analysis: Luna low minimal ×2.
  - Pipeline: Luna low minimal analysis, then Luna medium minimal and Luna low minimal beats, on single-player analysis cases ×1 (`pipelinePlan`). The baseline chain stays on every case.
  - Dry run of 2026-09-26: $2.38 isolated plus $0.18 for chains, against the $3 cap.
- Stage 4 (Milestone 3, the coordinator's carry-forward): the GPT-6-style rewrite. No analysis arms and no chains. It ran with `--prompt-state postfix`; a new run takes the current tag.
  - Setup on the 9 `STAGE3_SETUP_PREMISES` ×1: Sol low and gpt-4.1 (production's default settings), each `rewrite` and `rewriteZeroShot`.
  - Beats on single-player cases, in this order: Luna medium `rewriteSlim` ×2 (the lead), gpt-4.1-mini `rewrite` ×1 (does the cleanup help today's model?), Luna medium verbosity low `rewriteSlim` ×1, and Luna medium `rewrite` ×1 (the full-scaffold hedge).
  - Dry run of 2026-09-26: 256 jobs (setup 36, beat 220), $3.62 uncached against the $4 cap. Setup estimates read low (see Estimates), so about $4.1 uncached is the realistic figure, over the cap as the plan expected.
  - Stage 4b, the count fix (owner, 2026-09-26), planned first in `armsFor("4", …)`: Luna medium `rewrite2Slim` on single-player cases ×2, and Sol low `rewrite2` and `rewrite2ZeroShot` on the 9 premises ×1. Dry run: 88 turns est $0.24, 18 setups est $1.52 (budget setups by hand at about $0.10 each). The owner approved a $6 Stage 4 cap for it: every Stage 4 `--run` passes `--stage 4 --stage-cap 6 --over-target-reason "Owner approved 2026-09-26: fix the rewrite's forced counts, re-run Luna medium turns and Sol low setup"` and `--arms` with Stage 4b keys only (`gpt-6-luna@medium/rewrite2Slim`; `gpt-6-sol@low/rewrite2,gpt-6-sol@low/rewrite2ZeroShot`). Without `--arms` the run refuses to start: planning first only orders jobs within a role, so a role-only run would reach Stage 4's leftovers (gpt-4.1's open setups, the verbosity arm, the hedge). The flag lasts one invocation, and each use appends to `budget-overrides.jsonl`.
- Prices per 1M tokens are in `pricing.ts`, with the cost and estimate functions. Cost = (I − C − W)·in + C·cached + W·write + O·out, where O already includes reasoning.
- Estimates count the JSON schema as input (`requestChars` in `jobPlan.ts`), because OpenAI bills it: the production schemas alone are 4K to 16.5K tokens (setup about 14K, a 1-player beat about 4.5K, a 3-player beat about 15K).
- Until a new variant has `MIN_MEASURED_RECORDS` measured outputs, it borrows them: `estimateBaseKey` is walked until a key has enough (the verbosity arm → `rewriteSlim` → slim → prod; `rewrite2Slim` → `rewriteSlim` first). A trim writes less than its full form, so its estimate errs high. Without the borrowing, Luna medium would be priced at the §2.2 guess of 6,200 reasoning tokens (measured: about 1,600), and the cap would refuse a run that fits.
- Estimates ignore caching: every input token is priced uncached, so a Stage 4 estimate reads high when caching works.
- Input is estimated at 4 characters per token, which undercounts the JSON schema: it bills at about 1.8 characters per token. Setup input therefore reads about 6–7K tokens (28–32%) low per call (Stage 3 Sol minimal: 14.6–15.2K estimated, 20.4–22.3K measured), while beats are about right. A setup estimate is not conservative: check a setup invocation against measured per-call costs (a Sol or gpt-4.1 rewrite setup is about $0.09 uncached), not against the pre-run refusal alone.

**Isolated versus pipeline.**
- Isolated: every beat arm gets the same fixed analysis (the stored one, or the baseline's for built cases).
- Pipeline: a chain runs the analysis call, checks it with the game's plan check and applies the repaired plan with the production step (`storyAfterAnalysis` in `jobPlan.ts`), then runs the beat built from it. A plan the game would ask for again is applied as written, because the eval cannot retry; its `planUsable` check fails. The chain's summed latency is the analysis-turn wait the 60 s gate reads. Without a chain, the report adds the p95s of the analysis and the beat (conservative).

**Cases.** Built by `cases.ts` and `caseBuilder.ts`, frozen under `cases/`.
- **Stored continuations: 33 units.** A pregeneration `P(k,j)` is *adopted* when its latest beat reappears in `story.json` or any `P(k+1,·)`. Each adopted state, or `story.json`, is paired with every complete pregeneration made from it.
  - Measured 2026-09-26: 8988006e 18, 6edd813c 6, 7492b211 3, 2ee343b6 3, and the checkpoint 3, as the test plan expected.
  - The input carries the child's choice, roll and phases, so thread resolution is exactly what happened in play. The child's new phase is the fixed analysis. Its beat is the stored output.
- **Synthetic continuations: 5**, from unplayed snapshots. The choice is picked by hash and resolved at build time; 2 of the 5 have images off. The case state is always frozen past thread resolution: `resolveCurrentThreads` rolls dice (challenge threads) and is not idempotent, so it never runs at replay time.
- **Endings: 3.** `maxTurns` is set to the turn on a stored unit whose choice resolves the thread. Production reaches the ending through `getCurrentBeatType() === "ending"` after resolution. `determineNextBeatType` never returns "ending" at that point.
- **Built from templates:** 6 playable multiplayer templates (contest modes first) and 9 single-player ones. They give 3 single-player and 6 multiplayer first beats, 6 multiplayer continuations, and a switch and a thread case per template. Templates whose extra player slots have no characters or backgrounds are skipped.
- **Setup premises: 18** (`setupPremises.ts`). 13 are merged with a copy of the client's `buildMergedPrompt`, and 5 are reconstructed from stored custom stories. A test checks that no premise matches the blinding pattern.
- **Template iteration: 5**, with `creatorId` and `creatorUsername` removed (the prompt builder drops them as well). No stage runs them yet.
- **The 15-case subset**, hash-ordered: 9 single-player continuations, 2 first beats, 3 multiplayer and 1 ending.
- **Round cases: 17**, the ending's four built endings (2026-09-30, `--build-ending-cases`, above), and the stage scoping's `round-thread-first-8988006e-t1` (2026-09-29) and three last chapters (2026-09-30), all from `--build-stage-cases` (above) (`roundCases.ts`, source `round`, ids `round-…`, each note saying what it tests and how it was built; `--build-round-cases`, 2026-09-27, $0.024). The closed Stages 0 to 4 never plan them (`casesFor` in `jobPlan.ts`), so their rows and records stay as they ran; the migration check and the rounds do. A case frozen after later stages closed is planned only from its own stage on (`stagePlansCase`, `CASE_FIRST_STAGE` in `arms.ts`, in `STAGES` order): the stage scoping's case stays out of turn-rounds, migration and every feedback stage before stage-scoping (before 2026-09-29's fix it reopened 18 jobs there, isolated and chained).
  - Edited states (`EDITED_CASES`), all planning cases: two late switches at turn 8 of story 8988006e with 5 and 3 turns left (the last chapter then has exactly 4 or 2 turns, and 4 milestones are still needed); an outcome already at n of n there (the Waste Ring outcome at 1 of 1, the chapter that just ended adding an aftermath); a 10-turn story's second switch and second chapter plan and a 20-turn story's, with milestones scaled to the setup document's A10 budget (3 as 1/1/1, and 5 as 2/2/1 instead of the stored 6; at 10 turns the second chapter can only be 2 turns long, and its outcome is already complete); and three trigger rules in the setup document's new form (A7.1, A7.3): a stat at its threshold (Personal Agency at 40%, a rule and the stat's implication forcing a thread), a mid-story timing rule due at turn 9 of 20 on the one outcome no chapter has pushed, and an opening rule at the first switch of IPO to Mars (template 2db542e9) on its one-milestone outcome.
  - Played forward from stored GPT-6 replies: step 2 of the one exploration chapter (Café de Paris, `round-beat-exploration-checkpoi-t2`, from Luna medium's stored first step); the Neonate template's first switch plan and first beat from the production-default pair's stored chain, the feeding option chosen, then a new Luna low chapter plan ("A Discreet Arrangement", 2 steps: choosing the prey, then feeding, as the template's recipe asks) as a thread planning case and as the chapter's first step with that plan fixed; and the three-player Red Dust Rhapsody's first chapter played to its end (two new Luna low turns), then a new switch plan, switch turn and chapter plan (three independent threads), frozen as the switch plan, the switch turn, the chapter plan and the chapter's first step. `cases/round-build-report.json` lists what was built.

**Output folder** (`DOCS/2026-09-26_gpt6-text-eval/`, gitignored). The layout is listed in `evalFiles.ts`: `calls.jsonl`, `budget-overrides.jsonl`, `probe.json`, `cases/` (with `round-build-report.json`), `outputs/<callId>.json` (raw body, parsed output, metrics), `prompts/<sha256>.txt`, `rating/`, `keys/`, `scores/`, `results.md`, the filter check's `filter-check.jsonl` and `filter-check.md`, `check-baselines.md` and `.json` (`--check-baselines`), the preparation calls' `prep-calls.jsonl`, `chapter-frames.json` and `judge-calibration.md` and `.json`, the group judge's `judged-groups.md` and `.json`, the stage judge's `judged-stages.md` and `.json`, the ending judge's `judged-endings.md` and `.json`, the outcome-settled stage's `judged-settled.md` and `.json`, the recorded-result stage's `judged-recorded.md` and `.json`, the lever-direction stage's `judged-levers.md` and `.json` (v1's as `judged-levers-v1.md|json`), setup round 3's `setup-chain.md` and `.json`, and the playthroughs' `playthroughs.md` and `.json` with their pages in `stories/`.

**Blindness.**
- Item ids are `setup-NN` or `turn-NN`, and labels are A–D. The option order comes from `sha256(salt|item|arm)` with a fresh salt per page, and the baseline's position cycles across the labels.
- Every non-narrative string must pass `LEAK_PATTERN` (`blinding.ts`): the title, instructions, field labels, and context headings and labels. Those are fixed text; names, outcomes and plans from the story state go in the lines.
- The final HTML must not contain any arm key, model id, `@effort` or `/variant` from the key, nor the key's file name.
- Narrative is exempt from the word pattern: "Luna" and "Sol" are common names.
- The page is written only if nothing leaks. Keys go to `keys/`, never next to the pages.

**Gate readings in `results.md`.** These are readings, not verdicts. Each is marked within or over, no arm is dropped, and the owner decides which reading applies. Each post-fix section also prints today's production (the pre-fix baseline's per-story cost and setup waits).
- Modules: `pricing.ts` prices calls, `armStats.ts` computes the per-arm statistics, `gateReadings.ts` the readings below, `variantComparison.ts` the variant readings, and `stopRule.ts` the stop rule's moved reading they use. `resultsReport.ts` renders all of them and computes none.
- **Validity** (`validityGate.ts`), per isolated arm:
  - at least 98% valid on the first model attempt;
  - 100% valid within production's 2 retries;
  - worse than the same role's baseline only at a one-sided Fisher exact p < 0.05 on first-attempt failures. 1 in 100 against 0 in 115 gives p = 0.47.
  - Transport failures are left out, and `repaired` counts as invalid.
  - The diagnostic rates (repaired, refusal, length, text after JSON, junk) are read from first attempts.
- **60 s:** p95 at most 60 s, separately for beat-only turns and for analysis turns.
- **Turn waits per kind** (`turnWaits.ts`, the turn rounds' wait allowance under the stop rule, 2026-09-27): per prompt state, isolated beat arm, player count and turn kind (`turnKindOf`: first turn, chapter step, chapter opening, switch turn, ending; a planner case is the turn it plans), the p95 against 45 s on Luna medium and 60 s everywhere, a chapter opening against 60 s on every arm (coordinator, 2026-09-28: up to 60 s p95 if no slower than today's form, the second half read beside the table), marked within or over. A switch turn and a chapter opening wait for their planner too (turn doc A.C): a chain's measured wait where one ran with that beat arm (its planner the same key, else production's default for the role and player count), else the turn's p95 plus the planner's (summed, reads high). A turn alone waits for its whole job: an attempt re-sent at once (`PRODUCTION_RETRIED_OUTCOMES`, a reply cut at the output cap among them) adds its latency to the answer's, while a transport failure (retried after a backoff) does not. Hung calls and attempts cut at the output cap are counted apart, since no percentile holds them. `results.md` prints the table under each prompt state; `--check-baselines` uses the same kinds. Beside it, `turnWaitsBySample` reads each sample of an arm as its own run (its turns, p50, p95, the median milliseconds per output token and its hours), so a candidate reads against the reference sample that ran beside it.
- **Cost:**
  - The per-story cost against the baseline's on two bases: billed against billed, and uncached against uncached. Uncached is caching off: cache reads and cache writes are both priced as plain input. No record before Stage 4 has cache writes, so that changed no earlier figure.
  - With pregeneration that is 85 beats, 19 switch calls, 21 thread calls and 1 setup; without it, 29/7/7.
  - A call's cost sums its attempts.
  - Each role is priced at the story's player count when such calls exist, else at the mean over all calls. The summed analysis-turn wait uses single-player waits, since multiplayer calls carry more output.
- **Setup:** at most 1.5 × today's wait, read on the median and on the p95 side by side.
- **Multiplayer:**
  - Above the baseline p95 + 5 s, the arm is marked "needs multiplayer pregeneration"; above 60 s it fails.
  - The cost per custom story by player count is shown without multiplayer pregeneration (29/7/7 calls) and with it (85/19/21, an approximation: it pregenerates the last player's options), on both bases.
- **No pregeneration** (reported, not gated): flagged when the full-turn median is at most 5 s and its p95 at most 8 s. The full turn mixes beat-only and analysis turns at 15/29 and 14/29.
- **Variants against their reference (Stage 3 trims, Stage 4 rewrite, the rounds).** Each non-baseline variant arm is read against its reference arm (`referenceKey`) in the same group and prompt state; the baseline's records can be the reference. Both sides are read on the (case, sample) pairs both finished. The reference's other cases and its rare-failure samples are left out. Columns read reference → arm.
  - Rows: median visible and reasoning tokens, p50/p95 waits, $/call and its share of a single-player story (85/19/21 calls, one setup), first-attempt validity, and mean state counts per call. The counts are facts, new elements, introductions, interludes, stat changes, `words` (prose, image tags stripped) and `planChars` (the plan's JSON length) for beats; elements, stats, facts per element, and identities and backgrounds per player for setup; switches; threads; `longestWhitespaceRun` on every role; and the repair and note counts (`repair:<kind>`, `note:<kind>`) on beats, switches and threads.
  - Input and caching: cache lines, calls that wrote a cache, the cache read share, median input tokens, input $/call and $/call billed and uncached, and the 1-player-story share on both bases.
  - Noise: the reference's sample 1 against its sample 2 on the matched cases, for rule rates, counts and the design checks' pooled shares (`RATIOS` in `checkBaselines.ts`: outcomes naming an element, spendable player stats, effect numbers within ±15, shaped thread types, steering implications, and the beat shares). A one-sample arm (setup, gpt-4.1-mini, the verbosity arm) is read against a two-sample reference's noise; only a reference with a single sample there shows raw rates without a reading.
  - **The stop rule's "moved" reading** (owner, 2026-09-27; `stopRule.ts`, for every round from then on): a rule check or pooled share moved only when it is beyond that noise AND a one-sided Fisher exact test on the counts (`fisherGreaterP` from `validityGate.ts`: passes over replies, or a share's pooled numerator over its denominator) gives p < 0.10; a count moved only when it is beyond the noise AND at least 2 standard errors of the difference (each side's sample variance over its n; no spread on either side reads as certain). `ArmStats` carries the tallies (`ruleTallies`) and moments (`countMoments`) behind the rates and means. Each reading keeps `beyondNoise` (the reading before 2026-09-27) with its `p` or `standardErrors`, and `moved` when the rule holds. `results.md` lists moved lower, moved higher, and beyond the noise but not moved, each with its p or SE. A share's items cluster within replies, so its p reads optimistic; read it beside the checks that count replies. Before this rule, one setup in 36 against a zero noise moved a check.
  - Second references (`secondReferenceKeys` in `arms.ts`): a round's candidate read against another arm beside its own reference, marked "(second reference)" in every table but input and caching. Setup round 2's arms A and B against production's form (their base never ran alone, so a check counts against steering only when worse than both, and a form carried forward must be no worse than today's prompt on a check its round targets), and Sol low's round 1 against Luna low's (the templates question). The fix run: round 1b against round 1, round 1c against round 1b (the change it retests); round 2b's arm A against production's form, arm B against arm A and production's form.
  - `--results` rewrites `results.md` from the stored records and outputs, with no API calls, after a reading changes. The re-render of 2026-09-27 read setup rounds 1 and 2 under the stop rule; what changed is in `.plans/2026-09-26_build-followup.md` ("Setup rounds, stop rule").
  - Stored references: a reference is read in the arm's own prompt state, unless stored records of another state cover more of the arm's (case, sample) pairs. Stored records count only when today's code rebuilds their request byte for byte (`rebuiltToday` in `jobPlan.ts`: the record's `promptHash` against today's request for its case and variant), and only from the one state that covers most pairs. On a tie the arm's own state wins. The row names a stored reference `postfix:<key> (stored)`. A chain's records count step by step (since the final check, 2026-09-28): its planner step when today's code builds the planner side's request for the case, its turn step when the planner step does and today's code, applying that stored plan as the chain did (`storyAfterAnalysis`), builds the turn side's request byte for byte (`rebuiltToday(cases, { records, load })`), so production's chains read against today's pair's stored ones. So setup round 1, recorded under `round0`, reads against the stored `postfix` Luna low and Sol low production setups and their two samples' noise. It switches to the migration stage's `round0` records only once those cover as many of its pairs, so a migration run narrowed with `--cases` or stopped at its cap never replaces the full stored set. The hash covers the prompt text, not the schema; the setup schema's files have not changed since those records (git log, 2026-09-27).
  - Waits against a stored reference carry the drift between the two run days; the reference's two-sample noise has none, because its samples ran side by side. For setup round 1's wait criterion (at most +3 s on Luna low), read the median output tokens (visible plus reasoning) as the primary signal. At the stored Luna low setups' 6.9 ms per output token, 3 s is about 430 tokens. The p50 wait is read beside it.
  - Chains give the analysis-turn wait, and setup shows medians by player count.
- **Checks on what the game keeps** (`checksForRecords` in `outputChecks.ts`, since 2026-09-27). Beat, switch and thread replies are checked after the game's own repairs, as play keeps them: `repairBeatReply` (`beatRepairs.ts`) against the beat's input, `checkSwitchPlan` / `checkThreadPlan` (`planChecks.ts`) against the analysis case. The rule checks themselves stay pure over what they get (`textChecks.ts`); the eval's `optionType` rule is production's `expectedOptionType`.
  - `withRepairs` adds a count per repair kind (`repair:<kind>`, for example `repair:statIdSeatForm`) and per note (`note:<kind>`, kept as written, for example `note:offLadderValue`), the check `noRepairs` (notes don't count) and, for plans, `planUsable` (the check found no problem, so the game would not ask again). Every kind a role's replies show is counted on each of that role's replies, 0 where absent, so its mean reads per reply. The kinds are listed in `.plans/2026-09-26_build-followup.md`, "Round 0 play fixes (implementer)".
  - The model's raw compliance therefore reads as the repair counts, not as the checks: a seat-form stat change now passes `knownChangeIds` and counts `repair:statIdSeatForm`, a fact filed under a stat passes `knownIds` and counts `repair:factRefiled`, an option of the wrong type passes `optionType` and counts `repair:optionType`, and a topic switch with a junk fourth direction passes `topicChoices` after the cut. Reports written before 2026-09-27 read the raw replies.
  - Over the 2,660 stored outputs to Stage 4b (re-checked 2026-09-27, no API calls): 202 of 1,665 beat replies needed a repair (factRefiled 197, introductionDropped 74, statIdSeatForm 58, optionType 7, statGroupMoved 3, milestoneIdMapped 2, milestoneDropped 2, milestoneFromPlan 1; off-ladder notes 9), 5 of 366 switch plans (directionJunk 8, directionsTrimmed 1) and 4 of 303 thread plans (threadPlayerRepeated 5, threadDropped 4); no stored plan was unusable.
- **Game words** (`noMetaWords`, and `characterNames` on the same text) read what the player sees: the title, the prose with each image tag replaced by its `desc` caption, the options and the interludes. A portrait tag's `id=player1` no longer trips `player ?\d`, a caption that says "Player 2" still does.
- **Startable setups** (`startable`, a rule check on setups): the game's own start rule (`templateStartProblem` in `core/utils/outcomeReadiness.ts`) on the reply's shared and seat outcomes. It fails on a setup with no outcomes on the seats in play, or a multiplayer setup without a shared outcome, which the game retries once and then refuses.
- **Blank list items** (`noBlankItems`, a rule check on beat replies and setups, `blankItems` in `textChecks.ts`): passes when no list item anywhere in the reply is an empty or whitespace-only string, or an object whose own text field (`text`, `name`, `title`, `question`, `fact`, `newMilestone`) is. Other empty strings are legitimate (an interlude without a picture, an abstract element's appearance). Over the recorded outputs it reproduces the Stage 4 report's hand count: Luna medium rewriteSlim 14 of 88, Sol low rewrite 2 of 9 and rewriteZeroShot 1 of 9, and 0 on every other arm, the references included.
- **List counts** (rule checks in `textChecks.ts`). The worded rewrite has no `minItems`, so a model can drop an item instead of blanking it, and `noBlankItems` cannot see a missing one. Read the two together.
  - Setup: `storyElements` (6-8), `threeFactsPerElement`, `effectsPerStat` (at least 3 on every stat), and per player `threeIdentities`, `threeBackgrounds` and `threeOutcomes` (counting the shared ones). Counts: `factsPerElement`, `identitiesPerPlayer` and `backgroundsPerPlayer`, as means.
  - Beats: `interludesTwoToFour` (rule B32) beside production's `threeInterludes`, and the count `interludes`.
  - `effectsPerStat` reads production's "at least 3" contract; setup round 1 asks for two or three effects, so `setupR1` reads lower there by design, and the design check `effectsTwoOrThree` reads its rule (like `threeInterludes` against the worded beats).
  - Over the recorded outputs, facts, backgrounds, effects and the interlude range never miss. `threeOutcomes` misses most: gpt-4.1 rewrite 3 of 6 and rewriteZeroShot 4 of 5, Luna none prod 7 of 36, Sol low rewrite and rewrite2 1 of 9 each, and 0 of 36 on Sol low prod. Luna none prod has one 2-identity player and one setup outside 6-8 elements, and Luna low minimal one outside 6-8.
- **Whitespace padding** (`noWhitespacePadding` and the count `longestWhitespaceRun`, every role, `withPadding` in `textChecks.ts`): the longest whitespace run between JSON tokens in the reply text, read from `rawBody` (`loadReplyContent`), because parsing drops it. It fails above `PADDING_RUN_CHARS` (64); recorded layout runs reach 29. Over the records up to Stage 4 it flags 4 replies. One is Stage 4's 176 s Luna rewriteSlim turn (30,601 characters between two interludes). The other three are Round 1 production-form Luna turns, all on story 8988006e turn 4: 160,622 characters in a 295 s rare-failure call, and 60,352 and 31,710 in two chains. Each of those three padded after the beat had closed, before the reply's last brace. Stage 4b's rewrite2Slim adds 4 more in the same place, 3 of them on story 8988006e turn 4. Hung and cut-off calls leave no usable reply, so they read only as `outcome` `timeout` and `length`.
- **Design checks** (2026-09-27, the rounds' groundwork): the deterministic new checks of the setup document (Appendix B13, `setupDesignChecks.ts`) and the turn document (Appendix A.C, `turnDesignChecks.ts`), run beside the rule checks by `checksForRecords` (which also returns them alone as `design`) on the reply the game keeps. `milestoneNotCopied` alone reads the reply as written, since a milestone the game adds from the plan is a copy by construction. A check that doesn't apply to a reply (no outcomes, no contest, not an ending) is not reported on it. Word-list checks are heuristics: read them as rates against the noise.
  - Setup, the retests' check (2026-09-28): `noPlayerNamedStats` (no player stat's name carries a seat number or a player character's name, premise-named ones included, which `noSlotNames` lets pass by S8: Casablanca's "Fatima's Emotional Reserve").
  - Setup: `singlePlayerOutcomes`, `modeSlate`, `personalFloor` and `milestoneBudget` (M = max(3, min(6, round(turns/4))), setup doc A10), `noMirroredOutcomes` (seat numbers and identity names taken out, premise names included), `questionFormMatches` and the count `compoundQuestions`, `noIdentityNamesInOutcomes` and `noSlotNames` (premise-named players pass, decision S8), `scoreStatPerContest` (two-player contest modes: at least one shared opposites scoreboard and one per contested outcome, no per-seat counters), `noCoopScore`, `effectsInRange` and the share of signed effect numbers within ±15, `effectsTwoOrThree` (a two-player contest's scoreboard may carry one, its catch-up: A2.1's "one way to catch up" and round 1b's "one or two"; since 2026-09-27, which moved round 1's reading from 34 to 36 of 36), `noFormula`, `sacrificeNoBonus` (B13's bonus, risk or "might", plus "potentially", "possibly", "chance" and a signed number; not "points", "risky" or "may", which the A5 worked example and stats counted in points use for a certain cost), `noEngineEcho`, `noProgressMeter` (the contest's scoreboard exempt), `spendableShare`, `tooltipDisclaimers`, `noExampleCopy` (eight-word runs of the example block of the prompt the setup was sent, read from `prompts/`; production's example block is about three times the length of round 1's, so part of a drop against the production reference comes from the smaller block alone), `noExampleNameLeak` and the count `exampleNameLeaks` (B13's goblin leak: round 1's example stat names, and round 1b's "Fervor" for its "Energy", as a stat's name, and its world's names anywhere, in a setup whose premise lacks them; read against the reference's own rate, since "Energy" is a common stat name that round 1's field examples use too, so the check keeps counting it after round 1b renamed the example's stat), `outcomeNamesElementShare`, `noGenericAlternation`, `triggerRules`, `triggerStatsExist`, `ruleNamesStat` and the count `rulesNamingStat` (switch rules naming one of the setup's own stats, whole name or an opposites side; proposal 9's problem), `threadTypeShapeShare`, `triggerImplications` and the share `steeringImplicationShare` (implications naming a value and a thread they force or offer, of all implications), `noPronounOnlyFacts`, `hookFacts`, and effects, implications and adjustments per stat. Setup round 3's (2026-09-28): `distinctIdentities` (a seat's three identities are called by three names, titles set aside, unless the premise carries the shared name; a name opening with an article is a role), `twoCampContests` (three-player contest modes: a two-sided contested outcome, no three-path race, an opposites scoreboard per contest, no lead string), `kidsStatBudget`, `kidsPlainStatNames` and the count `kidsLongStatNames` (only on a case tagged kids: at most two visible shared and two visible player stats, none hidden, no name side longer than two words), and the count `rolePronouns` (S2); `effectsTwoOrThree` lets a three-player contest's opposites scoreboard carry its catch-up alone too. A setup's input carries its case's kids tag (`setupInputOf` in `jobPlan.ts`).
  - Switch plans: `directionsOneOutcome`, `directionsDistinctOutcomes`, `noCompleteOutcomeOffered` and `lateDirectionsOnNeeded` (the owner's "binding late": when fewer chapters fit than milestones are still needed, every direction on an outcome that still needs one; the chapter that just ended counts as pending). Thread plans: `threadOutcomeKnown`, `chapterFollowsPick` (the flavor switch's outcome, or the chosen direction's), `oneKindPerChapter`, `lengthAllowed` (turn doc A4: 2 to 4, and either the turns left or leaving at least 3), `firstChapterGroupsAll`, `contestStepsNameBothSides`, `noGroupInSinglePlayer`, and the count `chapterLength`. Beats: `sacrificeApplied`, `oneMilestonePerEndedChapter`, `noUnearnedMilestones`, `milestoneNotCopied`, `noSacrificeInSwitch`, `noOptionsOnEnding`, `noSequelHook`, `noStatReadouts` (a stat's name beside a number, "%" or "points" outside sacrifice and reward options), and the prose and option shares: paragraphs opening with You, a closing sentence that waits, a last paragraph that points at the choice, stock phrases, "the path ahead", anomaly words, option sets with the same first word, the same or near odds (within 5), a lever (sacrifice or reward), rewards among the levers, and a negative base on bonus options.
  - The owner's chapter checks (2026-09-28, on thread plans; `chapterQuestionChecks` in `turnDesignChecks.ts`): `questionNearerThanOutcome` (the chapter's question is not its outcome's again: `nearDuplicateOfOutcome` fails a question equal to the outcome's, or one holding at least two and at least half of the outcome question's goal words, its content words as crude stems with stop words and the story's names of people and things left out) and `milestoneKindConcrete` (`typeOfMilestone` is not empty and names no "milestone", "progress", "advance", "headway", "step toward" or "move closer"). The question check reads a plan's own question (planner v2's); today's plans read on their kind of milestone only. The kind check reads the reply as written where its text is stored (`checkThreadDesign`'s `asWritten`, parsed from `loadReplyContent` by `checksForRecords`), so planner v2c's blank kind fails instead of passing on the question its stored plan keeps in its place; a stored kind of milestone that is the thread's own question is a copy and is not read, so planner v2 and v2b, which write none (their stored kind is their question), have no kind reading, in isolation, in chains or in the setup chain. `--check-baselines` also reads every stored chapter once on its backfilled frame's question and its plan's kind of milestone (`chapterFrameChecks` in `chapterFrames.ts`, "Stored chapters" in `check-baselines.md`). Baselines (2026-09-28): the 21 stored chapters 13 of 21 and 6 of 21; today's form on Luna low (round0) kind 84% ±11 of 38 plans; gpt-4.1-mini 20-33%; planner v2 (round0, isolated) question 76% ±6 of 38, in chains 69% of 58, and no kind reading (the 90% the first baselines showed read its questions; re-run 2026-09-28 over the same 4,003 replies, the other kind and question figures read as before, and today's form in chains reads 87% ±5). Today's form, and the stored chapters it wrote, are planner v2c's only kind references. A hand read of the flags: of the 8 backfilled questions flagged, 7 restate the outcome and one ("What will Jasper and Emil uncover about the runes' origin?") is borderline; of the 13 passed, 3 still restate it in other words (a question that keeps one goal word, such as "expose", and adds the chapter's own terms); planner v2's flags are restatements ("Does Camille become a pillar of the community or remain on the sidelines?") or "a next step toward" the outcome, one race question borderline. Read as rates. No judged version: no hand-read calibration set exists for it (`JUDGE_CALIBRATION` holds turns only).
  - Each step once (planner v2e's fix, 2026-09-30), on every thread plan as the game keeps it (so on every form, today's included): `stepsOnce` (no thread lists a step twice) and the count `repeatedSteps` (`repeatedSteps` in `turnDesignChecks.ts`: a step repeats an earlier one of its thread with the same title, or the same question of five words or more with its leading "Label:" left out; case, punctuation and curly apostrophes folded). On the stage scoping's plans it finds the hand count exactly: planner v2d 4 of 46, v2c 1 of 46, the climax clause 2 of 6; production's own code in the final check (adopted1, planner v2c's request) 3 of 19, planner v2 2 of 38 (isolated, round0), today's form none.
  - An exploration step's options follow its results (the choice-result stage, 2026-09-30; `choiceResultChecks` in `turnDesignChecks.ts`): `explorationOptionsFollowResults`, on a chapter step where a player's thread explores, each option sharing the most content words with the step's result at its own position (the game records option n as resolution n), as `switchOptionsFollowDirections` reads a topic switch; a heuristic, the deterministic proxy of the judged `optionsFollowResults`, read as a rate.
  - The stage scoping's checks (2026-09-29), on thread plans that carry planner v2d's own stage list only (no other form writes one, so they have no baseline on stored plans): `stagesNamed` (one stage per intended milestone, none blank) and `stepsWithinNamedStage` (at stage k of n with k < n, no step's question holds a word only a later stage's name has: `goalWords` stems, the story's names and the words of stage k and before left out; a heuristic, the deterministic proxy of the judged `staysWithinStage`).
  - The owner's feedback of 2026-09-30 (`feedbackChecks` in `turnDesignChecks.ts`, on beats, per player): on a challenge set, `primaryStatsDistinct` (each option's main stat, its largest counted bonus as `BeatResolutionService` counts them, first two within ±15, the first on a tie, none without a positive one: no two alike, at most one option without) with the count `distinctPrimaryStats`, and `sameStatsOnlyRiskSets` (two normal options with the same counted stats, none alike included, and the same base points: only risk tells them apart; the owner's turns-r1b item 3 had +15/+5 on all three at base 0, safe, normal, risky); on a rolled chapter step, `chapterRewards` and `chapterSacrifices` (the chapter's with this turn's, `chapterLevers`), `atMostOneRewardPerChapter` (no reward where the chapter offered one), `secondSacrificeSets` and `unreasonedSecondSacrifices` (a second-or-later sacrifice in the chapter, and one whose text holds no reason word: because, since, before, unless, while, if, only way…; a heuristic for the hand read); after the first turn, `noReusedSentences`, `openingNotReused` and `reusedSentences` of `turnSentences` (`reusedFromPrevious`: a sentence of five words or more told again from the player's previous beat, the same words, an eight-word run or three quarters of the words shared; an opening with the previous beat's first five words, or a first sentence told again). Shares: `sameStatsOnlyRiskShare`, `secondSacrificeShare` and `unreasonedSecondSacrificeShare` (of challenge sets; arm O allows a second sacrifice for a strong reason, so the owner's criterion is the one without), `reusedSentenceShare`. Arm O's line reads as `leverFollowsChapterLine` (no sacrifice where `chapterLeverRule` gives none, no reward where it gives none; reported only where it forbids one), today's rate as `leverFollowsRateLine`.

No stored chapter offered a reward before its turn, so the reward cap binds on none of the 44 turns; arm O's strong-reason clause binds on 24 of the 32 rolled steps (above). Added after verification (2026-09-30, the same replies): `leverFollowsChapterLine` production 96% ±0 of 48, today's form 81% ±8 of 96, gpt-4.1-mini 88% of 48; `unreasonedSecondSacrificeShare` production 11% ±9 (the same 7 as `secondSacrificeShare`), today's form 41% ±3 (second sacrifices 47%), gpt-4.1-mini 52% ±2 (53%). The reuse checks read 10 flags over every stored GPT-6 turn, all ten a true re-narration of the previous beat's close by hand (six of them the café terrace's "As the afternoon sun warms the terrace", two a closing line copied word for word); in the stored play histories (gpt-4.1-mini), each case's last beat against the one before reads a reused opening on two stories' transitions: the owner's café terrace (`cont-6edd813c`, the switch into the chapter's first step, which saw only the switch's summary) and a Novi Reg chapter step.
    - Version O2's own readings (2026-09-30, read on every arm): `mainStatsDistinctLeverApart` (O2's stat rule: the main stats distinct, and at most one normal option without a main stat; a sacrifice or reward option is left out of that count, and a lever's own bonus still counts against the others), `leverFollowsO2Line` (no sacrifice where `o2LeverRule` gives none, no reward once the chapter offered one; reported only where it forbids one), and `rewardInvitedSets` / `rewardWhereInvitedSets` (rolled sets where O2's line invites a reward, the chapter having offered none, and those carrying one; share `rewardWhereInvitedShare`). Baselines (`--check-baselines`, the options and continuity run's replies under `adopted2`, 64 rolled sets an arm): `mainStatsDistinctLeverApart` production 23% ±3 (`primaryStatsDistinct` 17% ±9: the 4 lever sets with a bare sensible option pass O2's rule), arm O 39% ±3, OC 48% ±3, C 23% ±3, `turnOb` 0% of 21; `rewardWhereInvitedShare` production 11% ±3 (7 of 64: O2's line invites a reward on all 64), C 9% ±6, O 3% ±0, OC 2% ±3, `turnOb` 0%; `leverFollowsO2Line` binds only on `cont-8988006e-t5` (6 sets: no sacrifice, the rate), production 83%, O 67%, C 50%, OC 100%. O2 fails `leverFollowsRateLine` and `leverFollowsChapterLine` by design wherever it offers a reward its line invites and today's rate gives none (the 21 rolled stored steps after a chapter sacrifice, where both of those lines forbid a reward and O2's invites one), so a wrong-way move for O2 on those two is its invitation followed; its run reads `leverFollowsO2Line`, `atMostOneRewardPerChapter` and `rewardWhereInvitedShare` in their place. O2's retest reads `leverFollowsO2bLine` (no sacrifice where today's rate gives none, no reward once the chapter offered one; reported only where it forbids one), and fails `leverFollowsRateLine` by design only where it offers an invited reward.
  - Turn round 1's checks (2026-09-28): on switch plans, `directionsNoIds` (a round direction's own text, `topicDirections`), `lateOffersUntouched` (binding late: one outcome no chapter has pushed yet is offered while any still needs milestones) and `triggerFollowed` (a built trigger case's rule, `triggerCases.ts`: a flavor switch on the outcome it bears on); on thread plans, `questionPresent` (the chapter question names a story element or a player character), `planWithoutIds`, `kindFollowsOutcome` (A6's kind rule, the three-player race excepted), `kindFieldMatches` (planner v2's written kind against its results) and `triggerFollowed` (the Neonate recipe's two beats). A check whose field a reply lacks is not reported on it, so today's plans read only the kind and trigger checks.
  - Turn round 2's checks (2026-09-28), on beats: `switchOptionsFollowDirections` (on a topic switch turn each option shares the most content words with its own direction) and `switchOptionsNoIds`; `firstParagraphSpeech` (first turns); `endingAnswersEveryOutcome` and `endingNoMilestoneMixed` (only on replies that carry round 2's `outcomeEndings`); `leverFollowsRateLine` (no lever on a challenge turn where `sacrificeRewardLine` gives none; reported only there, for every arm). At the ending, `threeInterludes`, `interludesTwoToFour` and the facts, new elements, introductions and interludes counts are not reported, since the game never shows or reads them.

Setup, gpt-4.1 / Luna low / Luna medium / Sol low on production's form: `singlePlayerOutcomes` 0% / 88% / 100% / 63% of 16; `modeSlate` 85% / 50% / 75% / 60% of 20; `personalFloor` 58% / 97% / 97% / 81%; `noMirroredOutcomes` 55% / 50% / 70% / 95%; `scoreStatPerContest` 13% / 50% / 50% / 38% of 8; `sacrificeNoBonus` 0% / 22% / 44% / 86% (re-run over 2,664 replies after the word list was narrowed, 2026-09-27; it read 0% / 14% / 28% / 86% before, and no other figure here changed); `noFormula` 17% / 94% / 97% / 100%; spendable player stats 98% / 52% / 73% / 44%; effect numbers within ±15 86% / 92% / 94% / 65%; outcomes naming an element 49% / 65% / 58% / 60%; `triggerRules` 8% / 0% / 6% / 0%; thread types written as "(kind, n)" 0% everywhere; `noEngineEcho` 94-100% on production's form, 11-33% on Sol's rewrite forms. Beats, gpt-4.1-mini / Luna medium: `milestoneNotCopied` 13% / 100% (16 / 24 turns after a chapter or at the end), `noOptionsOnEnding` 17% / 78%, `noSacrificeInSwitch` 54% / 100%, paragraphs opening with You 32% / 23% (rewrite2Slim 9%), a last paragraph pointing at the choice 74% / 7%, levers in challenge sets 93% / 43%, three options at the same odds 0% / 15%; `sacrificeApplied` is 100% after the repairs (the raw losses read as `repair:statIdSeatForm`). Switch plans: directions naming one outcome 100% gpt-4.1-mini, 68% Luna low, 41% Luna none; thread plans: `chapterFollowsPick` 89% / 100% (gpt-4.1-mini / Luna low), `contestStepsNameBothSides` 0% on Luna (the last step says "both explorers"). `lengthAllowed`, `noCompleteOutcomeOffered` and `lateDirectionsOnNeeded` pass on every stored plan: the stored cases are not late enough to test them, and binding late applies to 22 of 36 stored switch cases only because today's setups plan more milestones than chapters fit.

Rank-1 setups name story elements in more outcomes (higher on 6 items, lower on 3), have more spendable player stats (5, 2), and fewer tooltip disclaimers (2, 4) and trigger instructions (1, 4). One rater and nine items: leads, not findings.
  - The stat-readout sample (31 hits over every stored beat) reads as real readouts ("personal agency at 60%"), so its false alarms are rare on this data.
- **The nearer frames** (the plan refresh, 2026-09-28; `nearerBackfillRequest`, `withNearerFrames`, `chapter-frames-nearer.json`): the same 19 chapters on the same view, asking planner v2c's question and kind-of-milestone fields in chapter words (`NEARER_QUESTION_DESCRIPTION`, `NEARER_KIND_DESCRIPTION`) and the plan; the steps and milestones stay fixed, and the stored kind of milestone is left out of the chapter shown (it is what the new one replaces). Keyed apart (`frame-nearer-<chapterKey>`); attached as `nearerFrames` beside `chapterFrames`, which stay as they are, so every earlier request rebuilds byte for byte. Only `chapterFullB`, the judge with `--frames nearer` and a page made with `--frames nearer` read them. `--check-baselines` reads them in their own section (the new question and the new kind; a blank kind fails).
- **Chapter frames** (`chapterFrames.ts`, 2026-09-27): the stored chapter plans were written on today's form, so they have no chapter question or plan (turn doc A3) and B2's chapter block would read in its fallback form.
  - A chapter is the thread plan a turn or planning case reads: its fixed thread analysis (a chapter-opening turn), else its state's latest thread plan (a step, the switch after it, the ending, a planner after it). Chapters are keyed by story and plan as planned (`chapterKeyOf`, step results left out), so the cases sharing one share its frame.
  - The backfill call shows the chapter as planned (outcome, milestones, steps, fixed) above the chapter planner's own view of the story, cut from `threadStep`'s prompt at `STATE_MARKER` (a production edit there fails its test: fix the marker, not the prompt), and asks for A3's two fields in A3's words. The view is exact where a chapter-opening case's own input exists (14 of 19); otherwise it is the earliest case state holding the chapter, cut back to the chapter's start (`stateAtChapterStart`: its turns and later plans removed, milestones and thread-type history recomputed; stats, facts and elements stay as that case has them, one step later at most). Those are the first chapters of the four stored stories and of one synthetic branch.
  - When cases are read, each that reads a chapter carries its frame (`chapterFrames`, per thread id) and the tag `chapterFrame` "backfilled", or "fallback" when its chapter has none, for round 1 to report apart. No production request reads either (a test holds it).
  - Run of 2026-09-27: 19 chapters framed (the 17 stored ones and the two new round chapters), read by 68 cases, none left in the fallback form; $0.011 on Luna low, about 4.4K tokens in and 200 out per call, often with no reasoning. One plan ends in a stray "R" (chapter `9086a93d6e3f`), kept as written.
- **Judged checks** (`judgedChecks.ts`, turn doc A.C, B2, B9): one Luna low call per player's turn asks `stepLeftOpen` (the options still decide how the character goes about the step), `concreteProgress` (something concrete changed that bears on the chapter's question) and `firstParagraphNarratesChoice` (the first paragraph narrates the last choice and how it went), each phrased so yes passes, evidence quoted first. Chapter steps get the first two, every turn after the first the third. The judge reads the player's title, paragraphs without image tags and options, the last choice and its result, and on a step the chapter's frame (or the outcome's question in its place) and the step. The prompt's version (`JUDGE_PROMPT_VERSION`, now 3) is part of each call's key, so a wording change is judged afresh and the earlier versions stay in the report.
  - Calibration (`JUDGE_CALIBRATION`, `--judge-calibration`, 2026-09-27, $0.068): the 28 player-turns the research notes read by hand (turn doc E6: S3-S14 and M3-M4 on gpt-4.1-mini and Luna medium), against the notes' verdicts; gpt-4.1-mini's progress, which the notes give only as a count, carries this session's reading (3 decided, 4 ambiguous), and M3's gpt-4.1-mini "settles" was corrected to ambiguous after the first run's results had been read (it is the next step played early; no reading changes either way). A check is judged reliably when sample 1 agrees with the hand on at least 85% of the hand-yes items and at least 85% of the hand-no items, with at least 3 on each side, and two samples agree on 90% (`isReliable`). The first bar (85% of all decided items, set before the runs) sat below the majority-class baseline: a judge that always answers yes cleared it on `firstParagraphNarratesChoice` (19 of 22) and `concreteProgress` (9 of 10). Each side on its own since 2026-09-27; no reading of the runs changed. The table and the hand-no items per check are in `judge-calibration.md`.
    - `firstParagraphNarratesChoice`: reliable on Luna low from prompt v2 on (v3: 19 of 19 hand yes and 3 of 3 hand no agree, the samples 25 of 25). v1 failed choices of direction (a switch option has no roll), which v2 words in. **Caveat:** its 3 hand-no items are all gpt-4.1-mini turns (S4, S5, S11) and every Luna item is a yes, so how often the judge passes a Luna turn it should fail is untested. Round 1 compares Luna against Luna, so its rate there reads as an upper bound until Luna negatives are marked (the owner's marks on round 1's page, below).
    - `stepLeftOpen`: reliable on neither judge in three wordings; the best is 13 of 17 (76%) on both, with Luna low's samples 17 of 18 alike. The misses sit on one boundary: a turn that shows a first move reads as the step carried out (Luna medium, v3), and a turn that performs the step but ends on an open question reads as open (Luna low, v1-v3).
    - `concreteProgress`: 7 to 9 of 10 agree, the samples 71-93%; only one hand verdict is a no, so this set cannot call it reliable (the bar needs 3 on each side) and a false pass is untested. The flips sit on two Luna turns where a conversation opened but no ally was won.
    - So round 1 can judge the first paragraph on Luna low, with the caveat above; the other two are readings only, beside the owner's rating (Coordinator's call, `.plans/2026-09-26_build-followup.md`).

**Known limits.**
- **Stored references against today's prompts** (`--check-baselines`, 2026-09-27): today's code rebuilds every stored `postfix` production-form setup request byte for byte (18 of 18 cases), so the stored setups are today's form and a setup round's pairwise page can read them as its reference. It rebuilds none of the beat (0 of 56), switch (0 of 18) or thread (0 of 15) requests: the Round 0 play fixes changed the state text every turn and plan reads. The turn rounds therefore read the migration check's `round0` records (2026-09-27, below) as their reference: today's code rebuilds every one of them byte for byte (the two `round0` beat records it doesn't rebuild are the round build's intermediate turns, on ids that are no frozen case).
- The built multiplayer and ending cases come from the baseline or from synthetic settings, not from real play.
- There are no dated GPT-6 snapshots, so a later rerun may meet a changed model.
- Run A (2026-09-26) did the probe, the case build and the pre-fix baseline for $2.39 of Stage 0. Its measured sizes, waits and per-story cost are in `DOCS/2026-09-26_gpt6-text-eval/2026-09-26_run-A-report.md`.
- The baseline's billed cost includes gpt-4.1's implicit cache hits, which depend on how close together the eval sends its calls. `results.md` shows the uncached figure beside it.
- Waits count each attempt alone, not the re-sends in front of it. If re-sends pass about 1%, the 60 s gate should read the summed wait per call.
- A call that hangs until the 300 s timeout is a transport failure. It is retried, left out of validity, and absent from every wait percentile. Round 1 had 4 such hangs in 714 Luna turn calls, so count them separately (`outcome` `timeout` in `calls.jsonl`).
- Stage 3's full forms are Round 1's records, so the trimmed waits carry server drift; tokens and cost do not. Multiplayer trims are unit-tested but not measured, because Stage 3 turns run single-player only.
- GPT-6's cache is measured at its default TTL: the eval sets no `ttl`.
- Multiplayer beats are not rewritten: the Stage 4 builder throws on them, and Stage 4 turns run single-player only. A multiplayer rewrite needs its own measurement before any multiplayer adoption.
- The rewrite keeps a few production descriptions that touch a prompt rule on purpose: the planning fields that ask the model to apply one (beat `optionConsiderations` and `newIntroductionsOfStoryElements`; setup `characterSelectionPlan.multiplayerCoordination`), the fields' own definitions (a story element's `instructions`, a fact's `storyElementId` allowing `world`, a character name unique across players), and production-internal repeats inside the schema (the stat's ±10/±20 effect sizes). They define or plan a field rather than restate the rule. Everything else a prompt rule restated is cut and pinned in the tests.
- The generic views pair a variant beat arm with the analysis arm of the same key (`configsFor`), else with the baseline's analysis, which is what every Stage 4 beat arm gets there. Read the variants from their own section.
- Round 1 (2026-09-26) ran the post-fix baseline ($4.03) and Stages 1–2 ($12.09), $18.51 in total. The owner's report is `DOCS/2026-09-26_gpt6-text-eval/2026-09-26_round1-report.md`. The rating pages are `rating/round1-setup.html` and `rating/round1-turns.html`; their keys are `keys/round1-*.json`.
- Until the Stage 3 run, the checks and pages split paragraphs on blank lines only. gpt-4.1-mini sometimes separates paragraphs with single newlines, so its post-fix paragraph and image-placement rates read low: 92.9% and 90.2%, now 100% and 96.4%. GPT-6 figures did not change. `round1-turns.html` was re-rendered with `--rerender-page`, and one option changed.
- Stage 3 (2026-09-26) cost $2.26, which brings the total to $20.77. The owner's report is `DOCS/2026-09-26_gpt6-text-eval/2026-09-26_stage3-report.md`. The Round 2 turn page is `rating/round2-turns.html`, and its key is `keys/round2-turns-891345004e.json`. No Round 2 setup page was built.
- Stage 4 (2026-09-26) cost $3.99 of its $4 cap, which brings the ledger total to $24.76. The ledger books its 43 hung GPT-6 calls at their estimate ($0.51); if OpenAI billed them as 300 s replies, the real figure is about $1.3 higher. The owner's report is `DOCS/2026-09-26_gpt6-text-eval/2026-09-26_stage4-report.md`. Its Round 3 page (v1, page id `291fb89390`) was superseded unrated: `rating/superseded/round3-turns-v1.html`, key `keys/superseded/round3-turns-v1-291fb89390.json` (`--score` finds a key in `keys/superseded/` since 2026-09-28, so a v1 export still scores). The verbosity arm and the full-scaffold hedge did not fit; gpt-4.1 setup ran on 6 premises with examples and 5 without (one job left open by the cap). Those three leftovers are still defined on the `exact` counts, so they would need `worded` versions before a run means anything.
- The Stage 4 rewrite's exact array counts (`minItems` equal to `maxItems`: interludes, facts, identities and others, with the count taken out of the description) make models pad: gpt-4.1 setups ran away in whitespace to the 32,768-token limit on 3 of 12 first attempts, Luna left blank items in 14 of 88 turns and Sol in 3 of 18 setups. GPT-6 also hung on 43 of 149 attempts, probably the same loop (not proven: a hang leaves no reply). `noBlankItems` reads the blank items, the list-count checks a list left short, and `noWhitespacePadding` a reply that padded and recovered.

The owner's report is `DOCS/2026-09-26_gpt6-text-eval/2026-09-26_stage4-fix-report.md`. The claim's completing milestone (turn 14) named the captain's win and the scoreboard ended 65|35, but that turn told it as provisional and stored facts saying it doesn't settle Pip's proposal (on top of turn 9's and 13's), and all three endings followed the facts; New Avalon's "renewed trust" came from turn 16 setting Orin's relationship to "Deeply Trusted" beside the parting milestone it wrote, which the stat-fit reading can't see. - The game's timing logs: none exist locally (no log file, and `data/stories` is empty), and `[TurnTiming]` exists only on this branch, so production has never written it. The share of single-player choices that find a finished pregeneration can come from production's older lines on main (`StoryProgressionService.ts`, in the host's log retention): per real choice, "Found complete pregenerated state for player1, using it" against "No complete pregenerated state found for player1, choice <n>". Multiplayer choices print the second line for every seat, player1 included, since pregeneration is off there, so the hit share this gives is a lower bound; the shorter "No complete pregenerated state found, generating beats" also prints on every pregeneration run and counts nothing.
- The `threeInterludes` check reads production's exactly-3 contract, while the worded form allows two to four (rule B32). A worded arm that writes 2 or 4 reads lower there by design. `interludesTwoToFour` reads B32's range, so a turn with 0 or 1 interludes fails there.
- The runner retries a timeout up to 3 times (`backoffsMs`), production twice (`PRODUCTION_MAX_RETRIES`), and validity leaves transport failures out. So a job that finished in the eval can still fail in production: 4 of 88 Stage 4 Luna rewrite turns answered only on a fourth attempt. The eval also waits 300 s and sends no output cap, where production cuts a turn at 90 s or at its output cap (12,000 tokens for one player; "Timeouts and output caps"), so a slow padded reply that finished in the eval would be cut off and retried in production (2 of 88 Stage 4b turns, at 24,500 and 39,530 tokens, past both limits; the other two padded replies stopped at 8,598 and 8,975 tokens within 68 s and would pass), and that retry is never observed. Read hangs per job from `calls.jsonl`.
